ZOTMPo–INAR(1) 过程:流行病学计数时间序列数据的熵和建模

ZOTMPo–INAR(1) Process: Entropy and Modeling of Epidemiological Count Time Series Data
作者： Manik Awale, Shrirang Pund, Hassan S. Bakouch, Aishwarya Ghodake, Amira F. Daghestani, Souha K. Badr
摘要中文翻译在本文中,我们提出了一种基于二项式细化的一阶整数值自回归(INAR(1))模型,其中创新序列遵循零一二修正泊松(ZOTMPo)分布。所提出的模型适应过度分散,并允许低计数值下的通货膨胀或通货紧缩,特别是在公共卫生计数时间序列中常见的零、一和二时。我们推导出模型的主要概率属性,并使用条件最大似然(CML)方法估计未知参数。进行蒙特卡罗模拟研究来评估估计器的有限样本性能。此外,我们建立了模型的信息论属性,特别是推导了香农熵和条件熵界,以量化随机过程的动态复杂性和可预测性。使用 登革热 发病率 和大肠杆菌 (E) 上的两个真实世界数据集说明了该模型的实用性。使用标准信息标准和预测准确性度量以及零、一和二的观测概率和拟合概率之间的欧几里得距离来评估模型性能。诊断检查,包括残差自相关、累积周期图和跳跃过程行为的分析,进一步确认了拟合模型的充分性。结果表明,所提出的 ZOTMPo-INAR(1) 模型为具有修改的低计数结构的过度分散计数时间序列建模提供了有效的框架。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究解决了改进统计模型的需求,以处理公共卫生 监测 中过度分散的计数时间序列数据,特别是针对 登革热 发病率 中常见的低计数事件。
设计与对象
作者提出了一种具有零一二修正泊松 (ZOTMPo) 创新序列的一阶整数值自回归 (INAR(1)) 模型,并将其应用于现实世界的数据集,包括 登革热 发病率。
核心方法
推导出模型的概率属性,并使用条件最大似然 (CML) 估计参数。蒙特卡洛模拟评估了有限样本估计器的性能,并建立了香农熵等信息论属性。
主要结果
结果表明,ZOTMPo–INAR(1) 模型有效地适应了过度离散和修改的低计数结构,并通过残差自相关和累积周期图等诊断检查确认了模型的充分性。
解释与创新
作者将该模型解释为为分析具有低计数通货膨胀或通货紧缩的过度分散计数时间序列提供了一个有效的框架,强调了其将 INAR(1) 结构与 ZOTMPo 创新分布相结合的新颖性。
科研与公卫意义
使用 登革热 发病率 数据证明了该模型的实用性,表明在 监测、预测和量化疾病 传播 模式的动态复杂性方面的潜在应用。
局限与证据强度
证据仅限于抽象级别的报告,缺乏详细的数值结果或跨不同流行病学环境的综合验证,需要进行适度的置信度评估。

ZOTMPo–INAR(1) Process: Entropy and Modeling of Epidemiological Count Time Series Data

Authors: Manik Awale, Shrirang Pund, Hassan S. Bakouch, Aishwarya Ghodake, Amira F. Daghestani, Souha K. Badr
Original abstract (English)
In this article, we propose a first-order integer-valued autoregressive (INAR(1)) model based on binomial thinning, in which the innovation sequence follows a zero–one–two-modified Poisson (ZOTMPo) distribution. The proposed model accommodates overdispersion and allows for inflation or deflation at low-count values, particularly at zero, one, and two, which are commonly observed in public health count time series. We derive the main probabilistic properties of the model and estimate the unknown parameters using the conditional maximum likelihood (CML) method. A Monte Carlo simulation study is conducted to evaluate the finite-sample performance of the estimators. Furthermore, we establish the information-theoretic properties of the model, specifically deriving the Shannon entropy and conditional entropy bounds to quantify the dynamical complexity and predictability of the stochastic process. The practical utility of the model is illustrated using two real-world datasets on dengue fever incidence and Escherichia coli (E. Model performance is assessed using standard information criteria and forecast accuracy measures, as well as the Euclidean distance between observed and fitted probabilities for zero, one, and two. Diagnostic checks, including analysis of residual autocorrelation, cumulative periodograms, and jump process behavior, provide further confirmation of the fitted model’s adequacy. The results indicate that the proposed ZOTMPo–INAR(1) model provides an effective framework for modeling overdispersed count time series with a modified low-count structure.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addresses the need for improved statistical models to handle overdispersed count time series data in public health surveillance, particularly for low-count events commonly observed in dengue fever incidence.
Design and population
The authors propose a first-order integer-valued autoregressive (INAR(1)) model with a zero–one–two–modified Poisson (ZOTMPo) innovation sequence and apply it to real-world datasets, including dengue fever incidence.
Core methods
The model’s probabilistic properties were derived, and parameters were estimated using conditional maximum likelihood (CML). A Monte Carlo simulation evaluated finite-sample estimator performance, and information-theoretic properties such as Shannon entropy were established.
Main results
The results indicate that the ZOTMPo–INAR(1) model effectively accommodates overdispersion and modified low-count structures, with model adequacy confirmed through diagnostic checks including residual autocorrelation and cumulative periodograms.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the model as providing an effective framework for analyzing overdispersed count time series with low-count inflation or deflation, highlighting its novelty in combining INAR(1) structure with a ZOTMPo innovation distribution.
Scientific and public-health significance
The model’s practical utility is demonstrated using dengue fever incidence data, suggesting potential applications in surveillance, forecasting, and quantifying dynamical complexity of disease transmission patterns.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited to abstract-level reporting and lacks detailed numerical results or comprehensive validation across diverse epidemiological settings, warranting a moderate confidence assessment.

Journal: Tropical medicine & international health : TM & IH  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-07  |  DOI: 10.1111/tmi.70213  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026
A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

媒介 登革热控制方法的比较有效性:系统评价和多级网络荟萃分析。

The Comparative Effectiveness of Vector Control Approaches for Dengue: A Systematic Review and Multilevel Network Meta-Analysis.
作者： Gary K. K. Low, Yuling Ke, Emmanuel Samson, Prutha Rane, Olaf Horstick, Carlos Alberto Montenegro-Quiñonez, Carlos Alberto Montenegro‐Quiñonez, 柯玉玲
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 登革热 媒介 控制在减轻登革热感染负担方面发挥着重要作用。传统的荟萃分析只能包括可以直接比较的干预措施。因此,本研究旨在通过多级贝叶斯网络荟萃分析直接和间接比较所有干预措施对登革热 媒介 控制的有效性。方法 包括整群随机对照试验(cRCT)和随机对照试验(RCT)。筛选和全文审阅由两名审稿人独立进行,如果存在分歧,则由第三名审稿人解决。所考虑的纳入研究的测量结果是昆虫学指数和流行病学措施。两名独立评审员使用 Cochrane 偏倚风险工具评估研究质量,并由第三名评审员解决任何差异。结果本研究共纳入48个cRCT和13个RCT。除两项具有低偏倚风险的研究外,cRCT 和 RCT 的质量总体上被认为具有高偏倚风险。网络荟萃分析和叙述综合表明,大多数干预措施并不明显优于其他干预措施。尽管网络荟萃分析缺乏统计上显着的结果,但独立干预措施似乎不如联合干预措施有效。结论 尽管在网络荟萃分析中并未发现大多数干预措施明显优于其他干预措施,但值得注意的是,单独干预措施的效果不如联合干预措施。这些结果强调了促进综合、多方面的 媒介 控制策略对登革热预防的重要性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
登革热媒介控制在减轻登革热感染负担方面发挥着重要作用。本研究旨在通过多级贝叶斯网络荟萃分析直接和间接比较所有干预措施对登革热 媒介 控制的有效性。
证据基础与方法
包括整群随机对照试验(cRCT)和随机对照试验(RCT)。筛选和全文审阅由两名审稿人独立进行,如果存在分歧,则由第三名审稿人解决。两名独立评审员使用 Cochrane 偏倚风险工具评估研究质量,并由第三名评审员解决任何差异。
共识与结论
网络荟萃分析和叙述综合表明,大多数干预措施并不明显优于其他干预措施。尽管网络荟萃分析缺乏统计上显着的结果,但独立干预措施似乎不如联合干预措施有效。
争议与缺口
除两项具有低偏倚风险的研究外,cRCT 和 RCT 的质量总体上被认为具有高偏倚风险。
科研与实践启示
这些结果强调了促进综合、多方面的 媒介 控制策略预防登革热的重要性。

The Comparative Effectiveness of Vector Control Approaches for Dengue: A Systematic Review and Multilevel Network Meta-Analysis.

Authors: Gary K. K. Low, Yuling Ke, Emmanuel Samson, Prutha Rane, Olaf Horstick, Carlos Alberto Montenegro-Quiñonez, Carlos Alberto Montenegro‐Quiñonez, 柯玉玲
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background Dengue vector control plays an important role in reducing the burden of dengue infection. Conventional meta‐analysis can only include interventions that can be directly compared. Hence, this study aimed to compare the effectiveness of all interventions for dengue vector control directly and indirectly through a multilevel Bayesian network meta‐analysis. Methods Cluster randomised controlled trials (cRCTs) and randomised controlled trials (RCTs) were included. Screening and full‐text review were performed independently by two reviewers, with resolution by a third reviewer if there was disagreement. Outcomes measured for the included studies considered were entomological indices and epidemiological measures. The Cochrane risk of bias tool was used to assess the quality of the studies by two independent reviewers, with a third reviewer to resolve any discrepancies. Results A total of 48 cRCTs and 13 RCTs were included in this study. The quality of cRCTs and RCTs was overall deemed at high risk of bias except for two studies with low risk of bias. The network meta‐analyses and narrative synthesis indicate that most interventions are not significantly superior to one another. Despite the lack of statistically significant results in the network meta‐analyses, a standalone intervention appears to be less effective than combined interventions. Conclusions Although most interventions were not found to be significantly superior to one another in the network meta‐analyses, it is noteworthy that standalone interventions were shown to be less effective than combined interventions. These results reinforce the importance of promoting integrated, multi‐faceted vector control strategies for dengue prevention.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Dengue vector control plays an important role in reducing the burden of dengue infection. This study aimed to compare the effectiveness of all interventions for dengue vector control directly and indirectly through a multilevel Bayesian network meta-analysis.
Evidence base and method
Cluster randomised controlled trials (cRCTs) and randomised controlled trials (RCTs) were included. Screening and full-text review were performed independently by two reviewers, with resolution by a third reviewer if there was disagreement. The Cochrane risk of bias tool was used to assess the quality of the studies by two independent reviewers, with a third reviewer to resolve any discrepancies.
Consensus and conclusions
The network meta-analyses and narrative synthesis indicate that most interventions are not significantly superior to one another. Despite the lack of statistically significant results in the network meta-analyses, a standalone intervention appears to be less effective than combined interventions.
Controversies and gaps
The quality of cRCTs and RCTs was overall deemed at high risk of bias except for two studies with low risk of bias.
Research and practice implications
These results reinforce the importance of promoting integrated, multi-faceted vector control strategies for dengue prevention.

共流行环境中基孔肯雅病毒血清学交叉反应:机制、诊断意义和流行病学后果

Chikungunya Virus serological cross-reactivity in co-endemic settings: Mechanisms, Diagnostic implications, and epidemiological consequences
作者： Ikeoluwa Feyisayo Aina, O. Opaleye, Dorcas Ayomide Atilade, Foluke Julianah Adetutu
摘要中文翻译基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 会引起急性发热性疾病并伴有关节痛,血清学检测仍然是其 诊断 和 监测 的核心。然而,在多种虫媒病毒共同循环的环境中,基于抗体的结果因交叉反应而变得复杂。本综述区分了影响 CHIKV 血清学 的三种相关但不同的现象:生物交叉反应性,由相关病毒之间真正的抗原重叠引起;与检测相关的非目标反应性,其中诊断平台在病毒之间没有建立生物学关系的情况下产生阳性信号;诊断错误分类,将两者混为一谈的下游流行病学后果。将该框架应用于五个病毒对,我们发现CHIKV-O'nyong-nyong病毒(ONNV)、CHIKV-Mayaro病毒(MAYV)和CHIKV-罗斯河病毒(RRV)关系得到了共享的甲病毒抗原结构的支持,并且在一些研究中还得到了功能性交叉中和的支持。相比之下,登革病毒 (DENV) 和寨卡病毒 (ZIKV) 相关血清中报告的 CHIKV 反应性缺乏既定的生物学基础,最好归类为未解决机制的检测相关非靶标反应性。这些区别很重要:未经调整的血清阳性率可能会夸大或掩盖 CHIKV 传播 的估计值,并且没有一个诊断平台可以解决每个病毒对的问题。我们认为,共流行环境中的 CHIKV 诊断 需要分层策略:急性期进行分子检测,随后进行抗原告知和多重 血清学,以及基于中和的确认(其中归因至关重要),并结合 监测 的交叉反应感知统计模型。加强分析验证和确认性检测,特别是在尼日利亚等 CHIKV、DENV 和相关甲病毒共同传播的非洲地区,对于提高诊断准确性和流行病学推断至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述研究了在共流行环境中影响基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 诊断的血清学交叉反应性,重点关注病原体 CHIKV、其疾病表现,以及如何改进基于抗体的检测以避免在多种虫媒病毒共同传播的地区发生错误分类的核心问题。
证据基础与方法
这是一篇叙述性综述,使用 CHIKV 和相关甲病毒(包括 CHIKV-O'nyong-nyong 病毒 (ONNV)、CHIKV-Mayaro 病毒 (MAYV) 和 CHIKV-Ross River 病毒 (RRV))研究的证据区分了三种现象——生物交叉反应性、检测相关的非靶标反应性和诊断错误分类,同时指出 CHIKV 反应性缺乏生物学基础。登革病毒 (DENV) 和寨卡病毒 (ZIKV) 相关血清。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,CHIKV 与 ONNV、MAYV 和 RRV 具有相同的抗原结构,并得到多项研究中共享的甲病毒抗原结构和功能性交叉中和的支持,而 DENV 和 ZIKV 相关血清中的 CHIKV 反应性在没有既定生物学基础的情况下,更好地归类为与检测相关的非靶标反应性。
争议与缺口
该审查强调,未经调整的血清阳性率可能会夸大或掩盖 CHIKV 传播 的估计值,并且没有一个诊断平台可以解决每个病毒对的交叉反应性,特别是在多种虫媒病毒一起传播的共流行环境中。
科研与实践启示
该综述建议在共流行环境下对 CHIKV 采取分层诊断策略:急性期进行分子检测,之后进行抗原信息和多重 血清学,基于中和的确认(其中归因至关重要),以及 监测 的交叉反应感知统计模型,重点是加强尼日利亚等非洲地区的测定验证和确认性测试。

Chikungunya Virus serological cross-reactivity in co-endemic settings: Mechanisms, Diagnostic implications, and epidemiological consequences

Authors: Ikeoluwa Feyisayo Aina, O. Opaleye, Dorcas Ayomide Atilade, Foluke Julianah Adetutu
Original abstract (English)
Chikungunya virus (CHIKV) causes an acute febrile illness with debilitating arthralgia, and serological assays remain central to its diagnosis and surveillance. However, antibody-based results are complicated by cross-reactivity in settings where multiple arboviruses co-circulate. This review distinguishes three related but distinct phenomena affecting CHIKV serology: biological cross-reactivity, arising from genuine antigenic overlap between related viruses; assay-associated non-target reactivity, in which a diagnostic platform generates a positive signal without an established biological relationship between the viruses; and diagnostic misclassification, the downstream epidemiological consequence of conflating the two. Applying this framework to five virus pairs, we find that CHIKV–O'nyong-nyong virus (ONNV), CHIKV–Mayaro virus (MAYV) and CHIKV–Ross River virus (RRV) relationships are supported by shared alphavirus antigenic architecture and, in several studies, by functional cross-neutralisation. By contrast, CHIKV reactivity reported in dengue virus (DENV)- and Zika virus (ZIKV)-associated sera lacks an established biological basis and is better classified as assay-associated non-target reactivity of unresolved mechanism. These distinctions matter: unadjusted seropositivity can inflate or obscure CHIKV transmission estimates, and no single diagnostic platform resolves the problem for every virus pair. We argue that CHIKV diagnosis in co-endemic settings requires a layered strategy: molecular detection during the acute phase, antigenically informed and multiplex serology thereafter, and neutralisation-based confirmation where attribution is critical, combined with cross-reactivity-aware statistical modelling for surveillance. Strengthening assay validation and confirmatory testing, particularly in African settings such as Nigeria where CHIKV, DENV and related alphaviruses circulate together, is essential for improving diagnostic accuracy and epidemiological inference.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines serological cross-reactivity affecting Chikungunya virus (CHIKV) diagnostics in co-endemic settings, focusing on the pathogen CHIKV, its disease manifestations, and the central question of how antibody-based assays can be improved to avoid misclassification in regions where multiple arboviruses co-circulate.
Evidence base and method
This is a narrative review that distinguishes three phenomena—biological cross-reactivity, assay-associated non-target reactivity, and diagnostic misclassification—using evidence from studies on CHIKV and related alphaviruses, including CHIKV–O'nyong-nyong virus (ONNV), CHIKV–Mayaro virus (MAYV), and CHIKV–Ross River virus (RRV), while noting the lack of biological basis for CHIKV reactivity in dengue virus (DENV)- and Zika virus (ZIKV)-associated sera.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that CHIKV shares antigenic architecture with ONNV, MAYV, and RRV, supported by shared alphavirus antigenic structure and functional cross-neutralisation in several studies, whereas CHIKV reactivity in DENV- and ZIKV-associated sera is better classified as assay-associated non-target reactivity without an established biological basis.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that unadjusted seropositivity can inflate or obscure CHIKV transmission estimates, and no single diagnostic platform resolves cross-reactivity for every virus pair, particularly in co-endemic settings where multiple arboviruses circulate together.
Research and practice implications
The review recommends a layered diagnostic strategy for CHIKV in co-endemic settings: molecular detection during the acute phase, antigenically informed and multiplex serology thereafter, neutralisation-based confirmation where attribution is critical, and cross-reactivity-aware statistical modelling for surveillance, with a focus on strengthening assay validation and confirmatory testing in African settings such as Nigeria.

哥伦比亚麦德林五个社区的埃及伊蚊中的沃尔巴克氏体 监测:对 监测 发布后的影响。

Wolbachia prevalence in Aedes aegypti across five comunas of Medellín, Colombia: Implications for post-release surveillance.
作者： Luisa M. Barrientos, Diego F. Rincon, Frank W. Avila, Catalina Alfonso-Parra, Catalina Alfonso‐Parra
摘要中文翻译减少蚊子 媒介 埃及伊蚊传播虫媒病毒 传播 的一个有前途的可持续策略是部署转染 Wolbachia pipientis 的个体,这种细菌可抑制登革热等病毒的复制,并被用于旨在阻止伊蚊传播的病毒 传播 的人口替代计划。2017 年至 2022 年间,世界蚊子计划发布了 Ae。哥伦比亚麦德林各地感染 wMel Wolbachia 菌株的埃及伊蚊。人口替换后的长期沃尔巴克氏体 监测 需要平衡准确性和成本的方法,同时提供有关目标人群感染持续性的可靠信息。因此,我们评估了 Ae 中当前的沃尔巴克氏体 患病率。埃及伊蚊,使用产卵器收集和成年蚊子的现场采样,对麦德林的五个 comunas(地理行政单位)进行了调查,横跨约 32.66 公里,并使用传统 PCR 和定量 PCR (qPCR) 评估了合并样本和单个样本中的沃尔巴克氏体感染情况。我们发现沃尔巴克氏体感染在调查区域仍然广泛存在,根据采样策略和分子检测方法,估计感染频率范围从约 60% 到接近固定。使用传统 PCR 筛选从产卵器中出现的汇集成虫,为确认沃尔巴克氏体跨位点的持续存在提供了一种经济有效的方法,而对单个现场捕获的成虫进行 qPCR 则提供了对感染 患病率 更敏感的估计。我们的结果表明沃尔巴克氏体广泛存在于伊蚊中。并展示了基于沃尔巴克氏体的 媒介 长期 监测 控制计划的实用方法。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究的重点是评估哥伦比亚麦德林五个社区埃及伊蚊当前的 患病率 感染情况,作为基于沃尔巴克氏体的 媒介 控制计划发布后 监测 的一部分,旨在减少 登革病毒 传播。
证据基础与方法
该审查基于 2017 年至 2022 年期间进行的一项研究,当时在麦德林释放了感染沃尔巴克氏体的埃及伊蚊。该研究使用了产卵器收集和成年蚊子的现场采样,通过常规 PCR 和定量 PCR (qPCR) 对合并样本和个体样本中的沃尔巴克氏体感染进行了评估。
共识与结论
研究发现,沃尔巴克氏体感染在调查地区仍然广泛存在,估计感染频率范围从约 60% 到接近固定,具体取决于所使用的采样策略和分子检测方法。
争议与缺口
该研究强调了对长期沃尔巴克氏体 监测 的需求,以平衡准确性和成本,但它没有解决麦德林不同地区或人群中沃尔巴克氏体 监测 的潜在差异。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,基于沃尔巴克氏体的 监测 长期 监测 控制计划的实用方法应结合传统 PCR 等具有成本效益的方法和 qPCR 等更灵敏的技术,以准确监测感染持续性。

Wolbachia prevalence in Aedes aegypti across five comunas of Medellín, Colombia: Implications for post-release surveillance.

Authors: Luisa M. Barrientos, Diego F. Rincon, Frank W. Avila, Catalina Alfonso-Parra, Catalina Alfonso‐Parra
Original abstract (English)
A promising sustainable strategy for reducing arbovirus transmission by the mosquito vector Aedes aegypti is the deployment of individuals transinfected with Wolbachia pipientis, a bacterium that inhibits replication of viruses such as dengue and is being used in population replacement programs aimed at blocking Aedes-borne virus transmission. Between 2017 and 2022, the World Mosquito Program released Ae. aegypti infected with the wMel Wolbachia strain throughout Medellín, Colombia. Long-term Wolbachia surveillance following population replacement requires approaches that balance accuracy and cost while providing reliable information regarding infection persistence in targeted populations. Therefore, we assessed current Wolbachia prevalence in Ae. aegypti, surveying five comunas (geographic administrative units) of Medellín spanning ~32.66 km, using ovitrap collections and field sampling of adult mosquitoes and assessed Wolbachia infection using conventional PCR and quantitative PCR (qPCR) in pooled and individual samples. We found that Wolbachia infection remains widespread across the surveyed areas, with estimated infection frequencies ranging from ~60% to near fixation depending on sampling strategy and molecular detection method. Screening pooled adults emerging from ovitraps using conventional PCR provided a cost-effective method for confirming Wolbachia persistence across sites, whereas qPCR of individual field-captured adults provided more sensitive estimates of infection prevalence. Our results suggest that Wolbachia is broadly established in Ae. aegypti populations in the surveyed areas and demonstrate practical approaches for the long-term surveillance of Wolbachia-based vector control programs.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on assessing the current prevalence of Wolbachia infection in Aedes aegypti mosquitoes in five comunas of Medellín, Colombia, as part of post-release surveillance for Wolbachia-based vector control programs aimed at reducing dengue virus transmission.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a study conducted between 2017 and 2022, where Wolbachia-infected Aedes aegypti mosquitoes were released in Medellín. The study used ovitrap collections and field sampling of adult mosquitoes, with Wolbachia infection assessed via conventional PCR and quantitative PCR (qPCR) in pooled and individual samples.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found that Wolbachia infection remains widespread in the surveyed areas, with estimated infection frequencies ranging from ~60% to near fixation, depending on the sampling strategy and molecular detection method used.
Controversies and gaps
The study highlights the need for long-term Wolbachia surveillance that balances accuracy and cost, but it does not address potential variations in Wolbachia prevalence across different regions or populations within Medellín.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest that practical approaches for long-term surveillance of Wolbachia-based vector control programs should incorporate both cost-effective methods like conventional PCR and more sensitive techniques like qPCR to accurately monitor infection persistence.

关于蚊媒幼虫监测员在提升社区环境卫生意识中的作用的宣传活动——南部城市 Metro Sumbersari Mardi Sentosa 保健站

Sosialisasi Peran Jumantik dalam Peningkatan Kesadaran Masyarakat terhadap Kebersihan Lingkungan di Posyandu Mardi Sentosa Sumbersari Metro Selatan

Authors: Noormawanti Noormawanti, Iswati Iswati, Muhammad Nur, Annisa Nur Firdausyi, Ismatul Izza Al Iftitah, Reni Wijayanti, Eka Susiani, Endang Rostatik
Original abstract (English)
Environmental health is one of the important factors in improving public health status, one of which is through the prevention of Dengue Hemorrhagic Fever (DHF) caused by the Aedes aegypti mosquito. This Community Service (PKM) activity aims to increase public knowledge and awareness of the role of Mosquito Larvae Monitoring Officers (Jumantik) in preventing DHF in the environment of Posyandu Mardi Sentosa, Sumbersari, South Metro. The activity was carried out through a participatory approach including an initial survey, planning, socialization, mentoring, and evaluation, involving Posyandu cadres, mothers with toddlers, and surrounding residents. The material delivered covered the role of Jumantik, the 3M Plus movement, and Clean and Healthy Living Behavior (PHBS). The results showed an improvement in participants' understanding of the importance of regular larvae inspection and the implementation of the 3M Plus movement, shown through participants' active involvement in discussions and their ability to re-explain the material presented. The outputs of this activity include a report, documentation, a publication article, and a Jumantik educational leaflet. This activity is expected to be an initial step towards a sustainable culture of clean and healthy living in the Posyandu Mardi Sentosa environment.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

太平洋地区的登革热:多国情况(截至 2026 年 8 月 7 日) - ReliefWeb

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 7 August 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报自 2025 年 1 月 1 日起,截至 2026 年 8 月 7 日,多个太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC) 已报告登革热 暴发,记录有 12 例 暴发。2026年受影响最严重的国家和地区包括美属萨摩亚、库克群岛、萨摩亚和新喀里多尼亚,这些国家和地区占确诊病例的94%。太平洋地区共有 21,258 例确诊病例和 21 例死亡,其中斐济 (n=8)、萨摩亚 (n=7)、汤加 (n=3)、纳奥罗 (n=2) 和基里巴斯 (n=1) 报告死亡。2026年1月1日至2026年7月2日期间,报告了4,511例确诊病例、173例住院治疗和3例死亡。登革病毒 血清型 1 (DENV-1) 和血清型 2 (DENV-2) 目前正在流通。库克群岛于2026年5月25日正式宣布登革热暴发结束,而汤加于2026年7月31日正式宣布登革热暴发结束。登革热暴发在瓦努阿图谢法省仍在持续,DENV-1继续传播。美属萨摩亚、基里巴斯、新喀里多尼亚、萨摩亚、瓦努阿图以及瓦利斯和富图纳群岛观察到 传播 水平较低。太平洋综合症 监测 系统 (PSSS) 迄今已报告 8,873 例登革热样疾病 (DLI) 病例,与去年同期相比呈下降趋势。
查看新闻五要素
时间
自 2025 年 1 月 1 日以来,太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC) 已报告 12 起登革热 暴发。2025 年,美属萨摩亚、库克群岛、斐济、法属波利尼西亚、基里巴斯、纳奥罗、萨摩亚、汤加和图瓦卢报告了登革热 暴发。截至 2026 年 8 月 7 日,登革热 暴发 仍在瓦努阿图谢法省持续流行,登革病毒 血清型 1 (DENV-1) 继续传播。2026年1月1日至2026年7月2日期间,太平洋地区共报告了4 511例登革热确诊病例、173例住院治疗和3例死亡。
地点与对象
太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC),包括美属萨摩亚、库克群岛、斐济、法属波利尼西亚、基里巴斯、纳奥罗、萨摩亚、汤加、图瓦卢、新喀里多尼亚和瓦努阿图谢法省。这些占确诊病例的 94%。
事件
自 2025 年 1 月 1 日起,多个太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC) 已报告登革热 监测。太平洋综合征 监测 系统 (PSSS) 迄今已报告总共 8 873 例登革热样疾病 (DLI) 病例,与去年同期相比呈下降趋势。登革病毒 血清型 1 (DENV-1) 和血清型 2 (DENV-2) 目前正在流通。
规模、影响与风险
太平洋地区共有 21,258 例确诊病例和 21 例死亡病例。斐济 (n=8)、萨摩亚 (n=7)、汤加 (n=3)、纳奥罗 (n=2) 和基里巴斯 (n=1) 报告了死亡人数。2026年1月1日至2026年7月2日期间,报告了4 511例确诊病例、173例住院治疗和3例死亡。2026年受影响最严重的国家和地区包括:美属萨摩亚、库克群岛、萨摩亚、新喀里多尼亚。
应对、状态与不确定性
美属萨摩亚、基里巴斯、新喀里多尼亚、萨摩亚、瓦努阿图以及瓦利斯和富图纳群岛观察到 传播 水平较低。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 7 August 2026) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
As of 7 August 2026, dengue outbreaks have been reported across multiple Pacific Island Countries and Areas (PICs) since 1 January 2025, with twelve outbreaks documented. The most affected countries and areas in 2026 include American Samoa, Cook Islands, Samoa, and New Caledonia, which account for 94% of confirmed cases. A total of 21,258 confirmed cases and 21 deaths were recorded across the Pacific, with fatalities reported in Fiji (n=8), Samoa (n=7), Tonga (n=3), Naoero (n=2), and Kiribati (n=1). Between 1 January 2026 and 2 July 2026, 4,511 confirmed cases, 173 hospitalisations, and 3 deaths have been reported. Dengue virus serotype-1 (DENV-1) and serotype-2 (DENV-2) are currently in circulation. The Cook Islands officially declared the end of its dengue outbreak on 25 May 2026, while Tonga has officially declared the end of its dengue outbreak on 31 July 2026. The dengue outbreak remains ongoing in Shefa Province, Vanuatu, with DENV-1 continuing to circulate. Low levels of transmission are observed in American Samoa, Kiribati, New Caledonia, Samoa, Vanuatu, and Wallis and Futuna. The Pacific Syndromic Surveillance System (PSSS) has reported 8,873 Dengue-like Illness (DLI) cases to date, showing a lower trend compared to the same period last year.
Original source text (English)

Time
Since 1 January 2025, twelve dengue outbreaks have been reported across Pacific Island Countries and Areas (PICs). During 2025, dengue outbreaks were reported in American Samoa, Cook Islands, Fiji, French Polynesia, Kiribati, Naoero, Samoa, Tonga, and Tuvalu. As of 7 August 2026, the dengue outbreak remains ongoing in Shefa Province, Vanuatu, with dengue virus serotype 1 (DENV-1) continuing to circulate. Between 1 January 2026 and 2 July 2026, a total of 4 511 dengue confirmed cases, 173 hospitalisations and 3 deaths have been reported across the Pacific.
Location and population
Pacific Island Countries and Areas (PICs), including American Samoa, Cook Islands, Fiji, French Polynesia, Kiribati, Naoero, Samoa, Tonga, Tuvalu, New Caledonia, and Shefa Province, Vanuatu. These account for 94% of confirmed cases.
Event
Dengue outbreaks have been reported across multiple Pacific Island Countries and Areas (PICs) since 1 January 2025. The Pacific Syndromic Surveillance System (PSSS) has reported a total of 8 873 Dengue-like Illness (DLI) cases to date, with a lower trend compared to the same period last year. Dengue virus serotype-1 (DENV-1) and serotype-2 (DENV-2) are currently in circulation.
Scale, impact and risk
A total of 21,258 confirmed cases and 21 deaths were recorded across the Pacific. Fatalities were reported in Fiji (n=8), Samoa (n=7), Tonga (n=3), Naoero (n=2), and Kiribati (n=1). Between 1 January 2026 and 2 July 2026, 4 511 confirmed cases, 173 hospitalisations, and 3 deaths have been reported. The most affected countries and areas in 2026 include: American Samoa, Cook Islands, Samoa, and New Caledonia.
Response, status and uncertainty
Low levels of transmission are observed in American Samoa, Kiribati, New Caledonia, Samoa, Vanuatu and Wallis and Futuna.

瓦努阿图登革热 暴发 情况报告#9(谢法省埃法特西南部) - ReliefWeb

Vanuatu Dengue Outbreak Situation Report #9 (South-West Efate, Shefa Province) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报正如 ReliefWeb 情况报告 #9 中详细介绍的,瓦努阿图登革热 暴发 在谢法省西南部埃法特报告了 9 例新的实验室确诊病例,使累计确诊病例总数达到 76 例。自 EW24 以来,暴发 一直保持在阈值之上,这表明 传播 社区仍在继续。总共报告了 12 例与登革热相关的住院病例 (16%),但没有 重症登革热 病例或死亡记录。暴发 主要影响 Efate 的多个地区议会,其中 Erakor、Pango、Eratap 和维拉港比例最高。响应活动,包括加强 监测、案件调查、媒介 控制、风险沟通和社区参与,正在进行中。该报告由 ReliefWeb 和南太平洋共同体 (SPC) 发布,提供了最新情况,但未提及未解决的信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于 2026 年 8 月 10 日发布,自 EW24 以来,暴发 一直持续进行,每周病例数高于 暴发 阈值。
地点与对象
据报道,瓦努阿图谢法省埃法特西南部出现 暴发 疫情,影响多个地区委员会,其中埃拉科尔、潘戈、埃拉塔普和维拉港比例最高。
事件
报告了登革热暴发,EW32新增9例实验室确诊病例,累计确诊病例达到76例。该报告由 ReliefWeb 和南太平洋共同体 (SPC) 发布。
规模、影响与风险
总共报告了 12 例与登革热相关的住院病例 (16%),但尚未报告 重症登革热 病例或死亡。暴发 正在与社区 传播 一起进行。
应对、状态与不确定性
响应活动,包括加强 监测、案件调查、媒介 控制、风险沟通和社区参与,正在进行中。该报告为情况报告,未提及未解决的信息。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Vanuatu Dengue Outbreak Situation Report #9 (South-West Efate, Shefa Province) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
Vanuatu's dengue outbreak, as detailed in the ReliefWeb Situation Report #9, has seen nine new laboratory-confirmed cases reported in South-West Efate, Shefa Province, bringing the cumulative total to 76 confirmed cases. The outbreak has remained above the threshold since EW24, indicating ongoing community transmission. A total of 12 dengue-related hospitalisations (16%) have been reported, but no severe dengue cases or deaths have been documented. The outbreak is primarily affecting multiple Area Councils on Efate, with the highest proportions in Erakor, Pango, Eratap, and Port Vila. Response activities, including enhanced surveillance, case investigations, vector control, risk communication, and community engagement, are ongoing. The report, issued by ReliefWeb and the South Pacific Community (SPC), provides an update on the situation without mentioning unresolved information.
Original source text (English)

继卫生部 - 皮内拉斯 登革热 警报后,皮内拉斯县加强了棕榈港的蚊子控制 - 皮内拉斯县 (.gov)

Pinellas County increases mosquito control in Palm Harbor following Department of Health-Pinellas Dengue Fever alert - Pinellas County (.gov)
新闻简报佛罗里达州皮内拉斯县在皮内拉斯卫生部于 2026 年 8 月 7 日发布 登革热 警报后,加强了棕榈港的蚊虫控制措施。2026 年 8 月 11 日发表的一篇新闻文章报道了该警报,该文章强调了该县对蚊媒疾病检测激增的反应。虽然没有提供具体病例或死亡人数,但该警报表明 登革热 传播 的风险增加。该县正在敦促居民采取防护行动,并正在采取措施减少蚊子数量。目前情况仍在调查中,随着蚊媒疾病检测的继续,预计将有更多细节。这一回应反映了该县对公共卫生和安全的积极态度。
查看新闻五要素
时间
继皮内拉斯卫生部于 2026 年 8 月 7 日发布 登革热 警报后,皮内拉斯县加大了棕榈港的蚊虫控制力度。该新闻报道于 2026 年 8 月 11 日发布。
地点与对象
佛罗里达州皮尼拉斯县,特别是棕榈港。受影响的人群包括有感染蚊媒疾病风险的棕榈港居民。
事件
皮内拉斯县响应皮内拉斯卫生部 登革热 警报,加大了蚊虫控制力度。这是根据官方通知确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
该报告提到蚊媒疾病的检测量激增,但没有提供具体病例或死亡人数。由于 登革热 警报,风险被明确声明为增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
皮尼拉斯县正在积极加大灭蚊力度,并敦促居民采取防护行动。由于蚊媒疾病的检测正在进行中,目前情况正在调查中。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Pinellas County increases mosquito control in Palm Harbor following Department of Health-Pinellas Dengue Fever alert - Pinellas County (.gov)

News Brief
Pinellas County, Florida, has intensified mosquito control measures in Palm Harbor following a Dengue Fever alert issued by the Department of Health-Pinellas on August 7, 2026. The alert was reported in a news article published on August 11, 2026, which highlights the county's response to a spike in mosquito-borne illness detection. While no specific case or death counts are provided, the alert indicates an increased risk of Dengue Fever transmission. The county is urging residents to take protective actions and is implementing measures to reduce mosquito populations. The situation remains under investigation, and further details are expected as the mosquito-borne illness detection continues. This response reflects the county's proactive approach to public health and safety.
Original source text (English)

印度首例登革热疫苗 Qdenga或于2027年抵达:它能阻止暴发吗?谁能接受?到目前为止我们所知道的 - livemint.com

India's first dengue vaccine Qdenga may arrive in 2027: Can it stop outbreaks? Who can take it? What we know so far - livemint.com
新闻简报India's first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, is expected to be available in the first half of 2027, according to the Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) and Takeda Pharmaceutical. The vaccine is a live, attenuated tetravalent dengue vaccine developed using recombinant DNA technology, designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes. It is indicated for the prevention of dengue in individuals aged 4 to 60 years. The World Health Organisation (WHO) reported a record 14.4 million dengue cases and 11,201 deaths globally in 2024, with 232,425 cases and 233 deaths in India. While Qdenga may not protect everyone, it is considered a potential tool to reduce the spread of dengue. The vaccine is expected to be available through private healthcare settings, and the WHO provides guidance on its use, including contraindications such as allergies, weak immune systems, and pregnancy. Healthcare professionals are advised to evaluate individual risks and benefits before administering the vaccine.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于 2026 年 8 月 10 日,并提到 Qdenga 预计将于 2027 年上半年推出。
地点与对象
印度,重点关注 4 至 60 岁的人群,特别是孕妇、10 岁以下儿童、老年人和患有合并症的人等高风险人群。
事件
印度首个登革热疫苗 Qdenga预计将于2027年上半年上市。它是一种活的减毒四价登革热疫苗,采用重组DNA技术开发,适用于预防4至60岁个体的登革热疾病。
规模、影响与风险
The WHO reported a record 14.4 million dengue cases and 11,201 deaths worldwide in 2024, including 232,425 cases and 233 deaths in India. Qdenga is designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes and may not protect everyone, with protection potentially decreasing over time.
应对、状态与不确定性
经过综合科学评估,疫苗已获得CDSCO在印度的上市授权。预计将于 2027 年上半年在私人医疗机构中提供。世界卫生组织提供了其使用指南,包括禁忌症和预防措施,并建议在 疫苗接种 之前咨询医疗保健专业人员。

India's first dengue vaccine Qdenga may arrive in 2027: Can it stop outbreaks? Who can take it? What we know so far - livemint.com

India's first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, is expected to be available in the first half of 2027, according to the Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) and Takeda Pharmaceutical. The vaccine is a live, attenuated tetravalent dengue vaccine developed using recombinant DNA technology, designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes. It is indicated for the prevention of dengue in individuals aged 4 to 60 years. Thu, Aug 13, 2026 SubscribeSign in View Market Dashboard Home Markets Market News Stock Markets IPO Mint Portfolio US Markets Mark to Market Bonds Commodities Cryptocurrency Market Data Stock Pages Index pages Commodities Gold Price Today Silver Price Today Fuel Petrol price today Diesel price today News India World WSJ Economist US News Trends Mint Portfolio Premium e-paper Global Partners WSJ Economist Barron's Mint Specials Mint Explainers Mark to Market Long Story Plain Facts Snapview Sections Companies Technology Markets Personal Finance Mutual Funds Insurance Auto Industry Lounge Opinion Latest News IPO Companies Start-ups Company Results People Technology Money Personal Finance Insurance Ask Mint Money Mutual Funds MyMint Mint Hindi More Technology Gadgets Tech Reviews Sports Cricket News Football News FIFA World Cup FIFA 2026 Schedule FIFA Points Table Science Health Entertainment Tools IFSC Code Finder Income Tax Calculator Gold Rate Today Gold Rate in Delhi Gold Rate Hyderabad Silver Rate Today Silver Rate in Delhi Silver Rate in Bangalore US US Trending Hollywood Business US Stock Market Explore Mint About us Print subscription Subscriber terms of use Privacy Policy Terms of Use Disclaimer Mint code Cookie Policy Code of ethics Sitemap Contact Us Hindustan Times Live Hindustan HT US News Nifty 50 Auto News Mint Money REITS India's first dengue vaccine Qdenga may arrive in 2027: Can it stop outbreaks? Who can take it? What we know so far Dengue vaccine Qdenga is likely to arrive in India early next year. Is this a new vaccine? Can it stop outbreaks? Who can take it, and what are the side effects? Here's all you need to know about Qdenga. Akriti Anand Published10 Aug 2026, 01:48 PM IST India's first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, is likely to be available in 2027.(HT_PRINT) AI Quick Read India's first dengue vaccine Qdenga is likely to be available in the country from early next year. Chandrakant Lahariya, one of the leading infectious diseases experts in India, said on his WhatsApp channel this week that Qdenga is expected to be available in India in the first half of 2027 "Qdenga is not yet available in India. Expected availability is in the first half of 2027, likely through a private healthcare setting," stated an infographic shared by Lahariya in his WhatsApp post on Monday, 10 August. Also Read | Doctors weigh in on the new dengue vaccine, Qdenga Takeda Pharmaceutical, the Japanese company that manufactures the dengue vaccine Qdenga, also said in July that it expects the product to be available in India in the first half of 2027, according to a Reuters report. Dengue is a mosquito-borne viral disease and one of the fastest-spreading vector-borne illnesses globally. The World Health Organisation (WHO) reported a record 14.4 million dengue cases and 11,201 deaths worldwide in 2024, including 232,425 cases and 233 deaths in India. What is Qdenga? Qdenga is a live, attenuated tetravalent dengue vaccine. The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare describes it as "a live, attenuated tetravalent dengue vaccine developed using recombinant DNA technology". Dengue is a disease caused by dengue virus serotypes 1, 2, 3 and 4. Qdenga contains weakened versions of these four dengue virus serotypes, so it cannot cause dengue disease. It is designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes, and is indicated for the prevention of dengue in children and adults aged 4 to 60 years. Is Qdenga approved in India? Yes. According to the government's press release on 21 July 2026, the Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) has already granted marketing authorisation for Qdenga in India. The approval was granted after a comprehensive scientific evaluation of the vaccine's quality, safety and efficacy in accordance with the provisions of the Drugs and Cosmetics Act, 1940 and the Drugs Rules, 1945. Also Read | India gets first dengue vaccine as CDSCO approves QDENGA Is Qdenga right for you? Who's at risk? Qdenga is the first dengue vaccine approved in India for the prevention of dengue disease in individuals aged 4–60 years. The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare said in a press release on 21 July 2026, “The vaccine is indicated for the prevention of dengue disease in individuals 4 to 60 years of age.” However, Dr Chandrakant Lahariya advises people to consult a doctor first to determine whether Odenga is right for them. Who's most at risk of contracting dengue: Pregnant women, kids under 10, the elderly, and those with comorbidities. Lahariya said that adults with diabetes, obesity, high blood pressure, fatty liver, high cholesterol, and other metabolic conditions "may be more vulnerable when serious infections strike, so prevention matters". Meanwhile, the World Health Organisation said that to make sure that Qdenga is suitable for you or your child, it is important to tell your doctor, pharmacist or nurse if any of the points below apply to you or your child. If there is anything you do not understand, ask your doctor, pharmacist or nurse to explain. According to WHO, do not use Qdenga if you or your child: 1. Are allergic to the active substances or any of the other ingredients of Qdenga. Signs of an allergic reaction may include an itchy rash, shortness of breath and swelling of the face and tongue. 2. Have a weak immune system (the body's natural defences). This may be due to a genetic defect or HIV infection. 4. Are taking a medicine that affects the immune system (such as high-dose corticosteroids or chemotherapy). “Your doctor will not use Qdenga until 4 weeks after you stop treatment with this medicine,” the WHO said. 5. Are pregnant or breastfeeding. Also Read | Japanese dengue vaccine QDENGA approved by European Union Warnings and precautions Tell your doctor, pharmacist or nurse before receiving Qdenga if you or your child: 1. Have an infection with fever. It might be necessary to postpone the vaccination until recovery. 2. Your doctor must carefully consider the risks and benefits of vaccination. 3. Have ever fainted from an injection. Dizziness, fainting, and sometimes falling, can happen (mostly in young people) following, or even before, any injection with a needle. When to get vaccinated? “If your doctor advises vaccination, it is best to start before dengue season or before expected exposure,” Dr Chandrakant Lahariya mentioned in his WhatsApp post. How many doses of Qdenga must one take, and when? Two doses complete the schedule. It is given as a subcutaneous injection in two doses at 0 and three months. A subcutaneous injection is a shot given into the layer of fatty tissue located directly below the skin and above the muscle. The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare said, “The recommended immunisation schedule consists of two doses of 0.5 ml each, administered at an interval of three months (0 and 3 months).” Can Qdenga prevent dengue outbreaks? Divya KS, an infectious diseases specialist at Apollo Hospitals, told Reuters, "Qdenga can help reduce disease severity and hospitalisations, but it is unlikely to prevent outbreaks as long as the mosquito vector continues to circulate." Meanwhile, the company said in February 2024, "Takeda's long-term goal for our dengue program has been to make QDENGA broadly available to those at risk who may benefit from immunisation." Also Read | Centre steps up dengue surveillance as monsoon raises outbreak risk Can you still get dengue fever after receiving the vaccine? Yes. The WHO says that, as with any other vaccine, Qdenga may not protect everybody who receives it, and protection might decrease over time. You may still get dengue fever from mosquito bites, including severe dengue illness, the WHO said in its advisory. You must continue to protect yourself or your child against mosquito bites even after vaccination with Qdenga. It says that after vaccination, you should consult a doctor if you or your child believes you might have a dengue infection and develop any of the following symptoms: high fever, severe abdominal pain, persistent vomiting, rapid breathing, bleeding gums, tiredness, restlessness and blood in vomiting. View full Image Information Credit: Dr Chandrakant Lahariya Possible side effects of Qdenga Like all medicines, Qdenga can cause side effects, although not everybody gets them. The following side effects occurred during studies in children, young people and adults. Very common (may affect more than 1 in 10 people): • injection site pain • headache • muscle pain • injection site redness • generally feeling unwell • weakness • infections of the nose or throat • fever Common (may affect up to 1 in 10 people): • injection site swelling • pain or inflammation of the nose or throat • injection site bruising • injection site itching • inflammation of throat and tonsils • joint pain • flu-like illness Uncommon (may affect up to 1 in 100 people): • diarrhoea • feeling sick • stomach pain • being sick (vomiting) • injection site bleeding • feeling lightheaded • itchy skin • skin rash, including blotchy or itchy skin eruptions • hives • tiredness • skin colour changes at the injection site • inflammation of the airways • runny nose Very rare (may affect up to 1 in 10,000 people): • rapid swelling under the skin in areas such as the face, throat, arms and legs Additional side effects in children 4 to 5 years of age: Very common (may affect more than 1 in 10 people): • decreased appetite • feeling sleep How does the vaccine work? Qdenga stimulates the body’s natural defences (immune system). This helps to protect against the viruses that cause dengue if the body is exposed to these viruses in the future, the WHO explained. How efficient is Qdenga? In a late-stage trial, the vaccine, approved in 43 countries so far, showed an efficacy of 80.2 per cent against confirmed dengue cases one year after the second dose, Reuters reported. It also showed 90.4 per cent efficacy against dengue-related hospitalisations after 18 months, meeting the secondary end-point of the study, according to the company. Who developed Qdenga vaccines? Qdenga is manufactured by Takeda GmbH, Germany, and will be imported by M/s Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India Pvt. Ltd. Is this a new vaccine? Qdenga is not a new vaccine. It is currently available for children and adults in 42 countries, including the European Union, the United Kingdom, Switzerland, Indonesia, Malaysia and Thailand, and has also received World Health Organisation (WHO) prequalification. Globally, more than 24 million doses of the vaccine have been distributed, according to the health ministry. In February 2024, the company reaffirmed its partnership with Indian vaccine maker Biological E to boost Qdenga's expansion as the tie-up looks to support production of up to 50 million doses annually. "BE will ramp up to a manufacturing capacity of up to 50 million doses a year, accelerating Takeda’s efforts to manufacture 100 million doses a year within the decade," Takeda had said in February 2024. The company's comment in July this year read, "We are progressing well and remain on track toward our goal of reaching a global manufacturing capacity of 100 million doses per year by 2030." 刚果-安哥拉边境发现登革热 传播 证据和多种伊蚊病毒组 - Bioengineer.org

Dengue Transmission Evidence and Diverse Aedes Mosquito Virome Found on Congo-Angola Border - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 8 月 10 日发表的一项研究报告称,刚果民主共和国和安哥拉边境沿线出现登革热 传播。研究人员在当地伊蚊中发现了多种病毒组,表明存在 登革病毒 血清型 1 至 4。研究结果强调了蚊子病毒生态系统的复杂性以及该地区登革热传播的诊断不足的可能性。虽然该研究并未证实 暴发,但它强调需要集成 监测 来监测登革热和其他蚊媒病毒。该研究结合了人类和蚊子的调查,利用基因测序来检测病毒谱系并评估 传播 风险。它还指出,与蚊子相关的病毒可能会影响登革热行为,强调了解当地病毒组对于公共卫生准备的重要性。该研究呼吁继续关注该地区,因为气候、城市发展和蓄水模式等因素可能影响伊蚊种群和登革热传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 8 月 10 日发表。证据中没有明确说明事件日期,但研究结果表明该地区正在进行 传播。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国与安哥拉边境地区。受影响的人群包括当地社区和伊蚊种群。
事件
刚果民主共和国和安哥拉边境沿线有登革热 传播 记录。研究人员发现了当地伊蚊携带的多种病毒,表明登革热传播的可能性。
规模、影响与风险
该研究强调该地区存在 登革病毒 血清型 1 至 4,这可能导致 重症登革热 轻度感染。研究结果表明,登革热可能正在传播,但未被识别为持续的 传播 链的一部分,这表明存在潜在的漏诊风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究提供了登革热监测的新证据,并强调了整合监测的必要性。它并未证实 暴发,但表明需要持续关注该地区的蚊媒病毒生态系统。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue Transmission Evidence and Diverse Aedes Mosquito Virome Found on Congo-Angola Border - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on August 10, 2026, by Bioengineer.org reports dengue transmission along the border between the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Angola. Researchers identified a diverse virome in local Aedes mosquitoes, suggesting the presence of dengue virus serotypes 1 through 4. The findings highlight the complexity of viral ecosystems in mosquitoes and the potential for underdiagnosed dengue circulation in the region. While the study does not confirm an outbreak, it underscores the need for integrated surveillance to monitor dengue and other mosquito-borne viruses. The research combines human and mosquito investigations, using genetic sequencing to detect viral lineages and assess transmission risks. It also notes that mosquito-associated viruses may influence dengue behavior, emphasizing the importance of understanding local viromes for public health preparedness. The study calls for continued attention to the region due to factors like climate, urban growth, and water storage patterns that can affect Aedes populations and dengue spread.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, August 12, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Dengue Transmission Evidence and Diverse Aedes Mosquito Virome Found on Congo-Angola Border by Bioengineer August 10, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Dengue transmission has been documented along the border between the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Angola, where researchers also identified a remarkably diverse collection of viruses carried by local Aedes mosquitoes. The findings, reported by He, Bobanga, Piantadosi and colleagues in Nature Communications, provide new evidence that dengue is circulating in a region where surveillance has historically been limited and highlight the complex viral ecosystems maintained by mosquitoes beyond the pathogens routinely targeted by public-health programs. Dengue is caused by four closely related but antigenically distinct viruses, known as dengue virus serotypes 1 through 4. Infection can produce anything from a mild, fever-like illness to severe dengue, in which increased vascular permeability, bleeding and shock can develop. The virus is transmitted primarily by Aedes aegypti and, in some settings, Aedes albopictus. These mosquitoes thrive in human-associated environments, breeding in small collections of stagnant water and moving efficiently between urban, peri-urban and rural communities. Discover more Infectious Diseases Health Conditions Health infectious disease The study’s focus on the Congo–Angola border is significant because border regions can act as epidemiological bridges. People, goods and animals move across them, while health systems and disease-reporting structures may differ on either side. In such settings, infections can circulate without being recognized as part of a sustained transmission chain. Detecting dengue virus or evidence of recent infection therefore does more than confirm individual cases: it can reveal an established, and potentially underdiagnosed, transmission ecology. The researchers combined evidence from human and mosquito investigations to assess whether dengue transmission was occurring locally. Human surveillance can identify exposure through antibodies, molecular tests or clinical patterns, while mosquito surveillance can detect viral genetic material directly in the insects that transmit disease. Each approach answers a different question. Antibodies may persist after an infection has ended, whereas viral RNA in a mosquito points more directly to recent acquisition of virus, although detecting genetic material does not by itself prove that every infected mosquito is capable of transmitting it. A central element of the work is its examination of the Aedes mosquito virome. The term virome refers to the complete collection of viruses associated with an organism or environment. In mosquitoes, this includes not only human pathogens such as dengue virus, but also insect-specific viruses, bacteriophages and other viral sequences whose biological roles remain poorly understood. Modern sequencing methods can recover fragments of this hidden viral diversity, allowing scientists to compare the viral communities found in mosquitoes from different locations and to search for known and previously uncharacterized viruses. This broader perspective matters because mosquito-associated viruses may influence the behavior of important pathogens. Some insect-specific viruses can replicate in mosquito cells without infecting humans, yet they may compete with, inhibit or alter the replication of arboviruses such as dengue. Other members of the virome could affect mosquito physiology, immune responses or vector competence—the ability of a mosquito to acquire a virus, support its multiplication and transmit it during a later blood meal. These interactions are scientifically plausible but often difficult to demonstrate in field samples, making the characterization of local viromes an important first step. The reported diversity also underscores how incomplete current knowledge remains about mosquito-borne viruses in Central Africa. A virus sequence recovered from a mosquito may represent a known lineage extending into a new geographic area, or it may belong to a deeply divergent group that has not previously been described. Genetic discovery does not automatically indicate a threat to humans. Determining zoonotic potential requires additional work, including genome analysis, laboratory culture where possible, tissue studies and epidemiological investigation. Nevertheless, cataloguing these viruses creates a baseline for recognizing future changes. Discover more health Infection infection Biological Sciences History For public-health authorities, the findings support a shift from disease response toward integrated surveillance. Dengue monitoring based only on patients presenting with fever is likely to miss infections because symptoms overlap with malaria, chikungunya, yellow fever and other febrile illnesses. Combining clinical testing with mosquito sampling, viral sequencing and environmental monitoring can provide earlier warning of transmission. It can also help distinguish imported infections from local spread, an essential distinction for deciding whether vector-control measures should be intensified around households, markets, transport routes or health facilities. The study also illustrates why genomic surveillance is becoming increasingly important in viral science. Sequencing can identify viral lineages, reveal genetic relationships between samples and detect changes that may affect transmission or diagnostic performance. Yet genomic data are most powerful when linked to reliable field information, including the mosquito species collected, location, season, host contact and clinical status of nearby communities. The Congo–Angola border investigation brings these strands together, showing how pathogen detection and virome research can illuminate the same transmission landscape from different angles. Evidence of dengue transmission in this region does not mean that a large outbreak is inevitable, but it does signal a need for sustained attention. Climate, urban growth, mobility and changing patterns of water storage can all influence Aedes populations and the movement of dengue virus. By documenting both the presence of dengue and the broader viral communities carried by mosquitoes, the researchers provide a foundation for more precise surveillance and future studies into how local ecological conditions shape arbovirus emergence. The message is clear: understanding dengue risk requires looking not only for one virus, but also at the mosquito ecosystems that allow it to persist. Subject of Research: Dengue transmission and the diversity of viruses carried by Aedes mosquitoes along the Congo–Angola border. 皮尼拉斯县报告两例登革热病例,为 15 年来首次

Pinellas County reports two cases of dengue, first in 15 years
新闻简报佛罗里达州皮内拉斯县于 2026 年 8 月 12 日报告了两例登革热病例,这是 15 年来首例本地感染病例。佛罗里达州卫生部证实了这些病例,其中帕斯科县蚊子控制区在西帕斯科地区发现了一例。登革热 是一种由埃及伊蚊和白纹伊蚊传播的病毒性疾病,会出现严重肌肉酸痛、关节痛、发烧、恶心、眼后疼痛、呕吐和皮疹等症状。没有具体的 治疗,卫生官员建议休息、喝水和服用对乙酰氨基酚来缓解症状。灭蚊工作优先考虑挨家挨户检查,并在棕榈港喷洒 治疗,以减少孳生地。虽然新病例的确切位置尚不清楚,但官员们将 监测 的重点放在那里。此外,哨兵鸡中的西尼罗河病毒有所增加,但今年没有人类病例报告。建议居民清除积水、穿上防护服并使用驱虫剂,以预防蚊媒疾病。
查看新闻五要素
时间
皮内拉斯县于 2026 年 8 月 12 日报告了两例登革热病例。发布日期为 2026 年 8 月 12 日。
地点与对象
佛罗里达州皮内拉斯县,特别提到西帕斯科地区和棕榈港作为重点区域。
事件
据佛罗里达州卫生部称,皮内拉斯县确诊了两例登革热病例,这是 15 年来首例当地感染病例。帕斯科县蚊子控制区在西帕斯科地区确认了一例 登革热 病例。
规模、影响与风险
皮内拉斯县报告了两例人类登革热病例。登革热 是一种由埃及伊蚊和白纹伊蚊传播的病毒性疾病。症状包括严重的肌肉疼痛、关节疼痛、发烧、恶心、眼后疼痛、呕吐和皮疹。对于登革热没有特定的 治疗,建议休息、液体和对乙酰氨基酚。
应对、状态与不确定性
灭蚊工作优先进行挨家挨户检查,并在棕榈港喷洒治疗,以减少蚊虫滋生区域。官员们将 监测 的重点放在发现病例的地区,但尚不清楚新病例是否专门发生在棕榈港。哨兵鸡中的西尼罗河病毒也有所增加,但皮内拉斯县今年没有报告西尼罗河病毒的人类病例。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pinellas County reports two cases of dengue, first in 15 years

News Brief
Pinellas County, Florida, reported two cases of dengue on August 12, 2026, marking the first locally acquired cases in over 15 years. The Florida Department of Health confirmed these cases, with one identified in the West Pasco area by the Pasco County Mosquito Control District. Dengue fever, a viral illness transmitted by aedes aegypti and aedes albopictus mosquitoes, presents symptoms such as severe muscle aches, joint pain, fever, nausea, pain behind the eyes, vomiting, and rash. There is no specific treatment, and health officials recommend rest, fluids, and acetaminophen for symptom relief. Mosquito control efforts are prioritizing door-to-door inspections and fogging treatments in Palm Harbor to reduce breeding sites. While the exact location of the new cases is unclear, officials are focusing surveillance there. Additionally, there has been an increase in West Nile Virus in sentinel chickens, though no human cases were reported this year. Residents are advised to remove standing water, wear protective clothing, and use insect repellent to prevent mosquito-borne illnesses.

A case of dengue fever has been confirmed in the West Pasco area north of Ridge Road, according to the Pasco County Mosquito Control District. ©Pawich Sattalerd The Florida Department of Health reported two cases of mosquito-borne dengue in Pinellas County Wednesday. Dengue fever is a viral illness transmitted through the bite of aedes aegypti and aedes albopictus mosquitoes, which are both found in Florida. Symptoms of dengue include severe muscle aches, joint pain, fever, nausea, pain behind the eyes, vomiting and rash. They typically present within 15 days of an infected mosquito bite. Advertisement Advertisement Pinellas County hasn't reported a case of locally-acquired dengue in over 15 years, according to data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. It is unclear if the new cases were identified in Palm Harbor, but health officials are focusing their surveillance in that area. The Hillsborough health department also issued a mosquito-borne illness alert Wednesday morning, announcing an eighth case of dengue in the county. There is no specific treatment for dengue. Rest, drinking fluids and easing symptoms with acetaminophen are recommended. Tampa Bay residents presenting symptoms should see a healthcare provider and contact the health department. Going door-to-door on Pinellas residents' private property, where mosquitoes are most commonly found, will be the priority of Pinellas County Mosquito Control's dengue prevention efforts. Advertisement Advertisement Mosquito control will conduct daytime backpack fogging treatments and door-to-door inspections in Palm Harbor. The inspections aim to reduce areas where mosquitoes breed, such as standing water in animal dishes, flower pots and buckets. Following the cases, mosquito control is advising Palm Harbor residents to increase protection efforts, such as removing standing water on their properties, dress in light-colored, long-sleeve clothing and closed-toed shoes and use insect repellent that contains DEET, Picaridin, IR3535 or oil of lemon eucalyptus. There has also been an uptick in West Nile Virus in Pinellas County sentinel chickens, the mosquito control reported. Officials will increase overnight truck treatments and daytime aerial treatments to combat the spread of viruses. Pinellas County has reported no human cases of West Nile virus this year. Dengue fever Hillsborough County: Two local cases prompt mosquito treatments - AOL.com

News Brief
Two confirmed cases of locally acquired dengue fever have been reported in Hillsborough County, prompting mosquito control officials to intensify surveillance and treatment efforts. Unlike typical travel-related dengue cases, neither patient recently visited an area where dengue is common, indicating local transmission. The cases occurred close together geographically, raising concerns about the presence of infected mosquitoes in the area. Mosquito Management Services is conducting ground inspections, mosquito trapping, and targeted treatments in East Tampa and near Picnic Island, while also using helicopters for aerial mosquito control for the first time this year. Residents are urged to eliminate standing water and take preventive measures to reduce the risk of mosquito bites. Officials emphasize that locally transmitted dengue is uncommon and requires an aggressive response to prevent further spread. The information was gathered from interviews with Hillsborough County Mosquito Management Services and an anonymous patient.
Original source text (English)
Dengue fever Hillsborough County: Two local cases prompt mosquito treatments The Brief - Two locally acquired cases of dengue fever have been confirmed in Hillsborough County, prompting mosquito control officials to step up surveillance and treatment efforts. - Unlike most dengue cases seen locally, officials say neither patient recently traveled, suggesting the virus was spread by mosquitoes within Hillsborough County. - Mosquito Management Services plans ground inspections, mosquito trapping and targeted treatments in East Tampa and near Picnic Island while urging residents to eliminate standing water and protect themselves from mosquito bites. TAMPA, Fla. - Two confirmed cases of locally acquired dengue fever in Hillsborough County have mosquito control officials taking action to prevent the virus from spreading further. County crews are preparing to treat areas of potential concern in East Tampa and near Picnic Island in South Tampa after determining both patients likely contracted the virus from mosquitoes within Hillsborough County. Officials say locally transmitted dengue is uncommon and requires an aggressive response. Hillsborough Dengue cases confirmed What we know One of the patients, who asked not to be identified, told FOX 13 he endured about two weeks of severe flu-like symptoms and spent four days in the hospital recovering from the illness. Unlike the travel-related dengue cases Hillsborough County typically sees each year, officials believe neither patient recently visited an area where dengue is common. Instead, investigators say both infections appear to have been acquired locally through mosquito bites. Because the cases occurred close together geographically, Mosquito Management Services says there could be one or more infected mosquitoes in the area capable of spreading the virus. Inspectors will be going door to door in affected neighborhoods to educate residents, while crews continue setting mosquito traps, collecting thousands of mosquitoes and testing them for viruses such as dengue. Local health officials respond What they're saying Mosquito Management Services spokesperson Gabby Henderson said most dengue cases identified in Hillsborough County are linked to international travel. "Typically, we see a couple cases a year that are travel-related, meaning somebody was in another country, and they came here and developed symptoms that got tested. Because these people have not traveled to any areas they've been staying within our county, that means they got bit by a mosquito that was carrying dengue in Hillsborough County. And because they are close together, that shows us that there may be a mosquito or multiple mosquitoes in that area that are able to spread this virus." Aerial mosquito treatment plans What's next This year also marks the first time Hillsborough County will be able to use its own helicopters to treat adult mosquitoes, rather than relying on outside contractors for aerial adult mosquito control. Officials say that added capability should allow crews to respond more quickly when mosquito-borne disease concerns arise. Residents are encouraged to eliminate standing water around their homes, wear long sleeves when possible and use mosquito repellent containing DEET to reduce their risk of being bitten. Officials also remind residents that even a bottle cap full of standing water can provide enough space for mosquitoes to breed. The Source The information in this story was gathered from FOX 13 News interviews with Hillsborough County Mosquito Management Services, as well as an anonymous dengue fever patient

新闻简报坦帕湾地区的 登革热 病例有所增加,截至 2026 年 8 月 12 日报告了 10 例本地感染病例。FOX 13 Tampa Bay 的新闻报道强调了 暴发,该报道指出病例有所增加,但未具体说明死亡或接触情况。该报告还包括其他当地新闻,例如海恩斯市的学校封锁和皮尼拉斯县的洪水项目投票。然而,登革热 更新是主要焦点,表明公共卫生当局正在监测情况。所提供的证据中未明确说明 暴发 的风险级别。该报告发布于 2026 年 8 月 12 日,虽然当天报告了病例,但没有明确确定事件日期。证据中没有详细说明具体的应对措施。

德里必须在季风登革热面前保持领先:为什么降雨、积水和延迟的 诊断 会使季节性威胁变得严重 - 印度时报

Delhi must stay ahead of dengue this monsoon: Why rain, stagnant water and delayed diagnosis can turn a seasonal threat serious - The Times of India

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Delhi must stay ahead of dengue this monsoon: Why rain, stagnant water and delayed diagnosis can turn a seasonal threat serious - The Times of India

News Brief
The article, published on August 8, 2026, by The Times of India, highlights the growing concern of dengue in Delhi during the monsoon season. It explains that the Aedes aegypti mosquito, which spreads dengue, thrives in stagnant water, often found in small containers like flowerpots, discarded items, or uncovered tanks. The piece notes that while dengue cases in 2026 are lower than last year, they are increasing due to favorable breeding conditions. Early diagnosis is emphasized, as delayed recognition can lead to severe complications such as plasma leakage, shock, and organ failure. The article also stresses the importance of public awareness, including covering water containers and seeking medical help promptly if symptoms like severe abdominal pain, repeated vomiting, or unusual tiredness appear. It underscores that dengue prevention is a local responsibility, requiring vigilance in identifying and eliminating small water sources. The piece serves as a general informative guide rather than reporting confirmed outbreaks or specific case numbers.
Original source text (English)
Edition IN IN US GCC English English हिन्दी मराठी ಕನ್ನಡ தமிழ் বাংলা മലയാളം తెలుగు ગુજરાતી Weather Sign In TOI Health Videos City Metro Cities mumbai delhi bengaluru Hyderabad kolkata chennai Other Cities City Crime News agra agartala ahmedabad ajmer amaravati amritsar bareilly bhubaneswar bhopal chandigarh chhatrapati sambhajinagar coimbatore cuttack dehradun erode faridabad ghaziabad goa gurgaon guwahati hubballi imphal indore itanagar jaipur jammu jamshedpur jodhpur kanpur kochi kohima kolhapur kozhikode ludhiana lucknow madurai mangaluru meerut mumbai region mysuru nagpur nashik navi mumbai noida patna prayagraj puducherry pune raipur rajkot ranchi thane salem shillong shimla srinagar surat trichy thiruvananthapuram udaipur vadodara varanasi vijayawada visakhapatnam photos Web Stories India featured Gadgets Now Urban Safety Maharashtra Delhi Karnataka Telangana West Bengal Tamil Nadu Andhra Pradesh Uttar Pradesh Gujarat Rajasthan Punjab Odisha Madhya Pradesh Haryana Tripura Uttarakhand Bihar Kerala Assam Arunachal Pradesh Manipur Chhattisgarh Jammu Kashmir Nagaland Jharkhand Meghalaya Himachal Pradesh Web Stories Digital Arrest Operation Sindoor Cancel Patriarchy Lets D Stress World Business Tech Gadgets Now Cricket Sports Entertainment Astro TOI Women TV Education Life & Style Web Series Times Evoke Photos Blogs economy TOI Exclusive Celebrations TOI Games Today's ePaper Live News Health News Delhi must stay ahead of dengue this monsoon: Why rain, stagnant water and delayed diagnosis can turn a seasonal threat serious Delhi must stay ahead of dengue this monsoon: Why rain, stagnant water and delayed diagnosis can turn a seasonal threat serious Aadya Jha / TIMESOFINDIA.COM / Aug 8, 2026, 17:30 IST Share AA +Text Size Small Medium Large Follow us 1/6 How Delhi can stay ahead of dengue this monsoon Delhi’s monsoon has a familiar pattern. Rain arrives, temperatures fluctuate, humidity rises, and water begins collecting in places that may go unnoticed for days. Behind this everyday change in the city’s environment lies another seasonal concern: dengue. The infection is spread mainly by the bite of infected Aedes aegypti mosquitoes. These mosquitoes are particularly comfortable around human habitation and can breed in surprisingly small collections of water. A flowerpot tray, discarded container, old tyre, uncovered tank or even a neglected cooler can become part of the problem. According to Dr Prakhar Singh, Co-Founder, OSVI Healthcare and Advisor, Mediways Health Foundation, dengue is once again a concern for Delhi with the arrival of the monsoon. He said that while the latest MCD figures show that the number of dengue infections recorded in 2026 remains below the corresponding period last year, cases are steadily increasing as rainfall and humidity create favourable breeding conditions for Aedes aegypti. That makes the timing of prevention important. Waiting for dengue cases to rise sharply before acting can leave communities trying to control mosquitoes after breeding has already expanded. 2/6 Why Delhi's monsoon creates the right conditions for dengue Dengue does not spread simply because it rains. The bigger issue is what happens after the rain. Water can collect in hundreds of small containers around homes, offices, construction sites and neighbourhoods. Aedes aegypti mosquitoes lay their eggs in water-filled containers, often close to people. Warm temperatures and high humidity also support mosquito survival and increase the possibility of transmission. WHO identifies rainfall, temperature and humidity among environmental factors that can influence dengue risk. There is another detail that often gets overlooked. The dengue mosquito is mainly a daytime biter. Its activity is particularly high in the early morning and before dusk. So staying indoors during the night does not automatically protect someone from dengue. This is why a rainy balcony, rooftop or courtyard deserves as much attention as a large waterlogged area. The mosquito does not need a lake to reproduce. 3/6 The first few days can look like an ordinary viral fever One reason dengue can be underestimated is that its early symptoms are not always distinctive. High fever, headache, body ache and fatigue can easily be mistaken for flu or another viral infection. Dengue can also cause pain behind the eyes, nausea, vomiting, joint or muscle pain and sometimes a rash. Symptoms usually begin four to 10 days after infection and can last for several days. This becomes especially relevant in Delhi during the monsoon because several infections circulate at the same time. Dr Inder Mohan Chugh, Vice Chairman and HOD, Pulmonology, Yatharth Super Speciality Hospital, Model Town, New Delhi, said fluctuating weather conditions can also bring an increase in flu cases. “The first thing to do to prevent infection from the virus is to keep away from crowds, maintain proper hand hygiene by keeping yourself clean all the time, eat healthy food, and stay hydrated,” he said. “In case you experience symptoms such as coughing, cold, fever, sore throat, and body pains, it is best to visit a doctor right away.” The distinction matters. Flu is caused by influenza viruses, including strains such as H1N1 and H3N2, while dengue is caused by the dengue virus and transmitted by mosquitoes. A fever during the monsoon should therefore not automatically be labelled as flu or dengue without medical assessment. Dr Chugh also said people at higher risk of complications from influenza, including those with chronic lung disease, diabetes, heart or kidney disease, cancer or weakened immunity, should discuss annual flu vaccination with their doctor. For dengue, the important warning is slightly different. A person may actually become more vulnerable to severe disease when the fever starts coming down. WHO says the critical phase can occur around three to seven days after the illness begins, meaning a falling temperature should not always be taken as proof that the danger has passed. 4/6 When dengue stops being “just a fever” Most dengue infections are mild, but a small proportion can progress to severe dengue. That progression can involve plasma leakage, severe bleeding, shock and organ impairment. Dr Singh cautioned that delayed diagnosis can allow dengue to become severe, particularly when warning signs are missed. Children, older adults and people with other medical conditions may require closer attention. Severe abdominal pain, repeated vomiting, rapid breathing, bleeding from the gums or nose, blood in vomit or stool, unusual tiredness or restlessness, extreme thirst, and pale or cold skin are warning signs that need urgent medical attention. Another important point is the use of pain medicines. WHO advises people with dengue to avoid aspirin and ibuprofen because these medicines can increase the risk of bleeding. Paracetamol is generally used for fever and pain, but treatment decisions should be guided by a doctor. There is no specific antiviral treatment that cures dengue. Early recognition, monitoring and appropriate medical care are central to preventing severe outcomes. 5/6 The most useful dengue prevention may be sitting unnoticed at home Dengue prevention often sounds like a large civic problem. It is one, but it is also remarkably local. A household does not need to find a large pool of stagnant water to make a difference. The more useful habit is a regular search for small water-holding spots. Dr Singh advised households to check coolers, plant pots, buckets, scrap tyres and rooftop tanks and ensure that water does not remain stagnant. Water-storage containers should be covered, while items that can collect rainwater should be emptied, cleaned or discarded. The check should extend beyond the obvious. Look under plant pots. Check unused containers on balconies. Inspect rooftop corners after rain. Make sure tanks are properly covered. Do not assume that clean-looking water is harmless simply because it does not smell or appear dirty. Personal protection still matters. Full-sleeved clothing, appropriate mosquito repellents, window screens and other measures can reduce bites. Since Aedes mosquitoes bite during the day, these precautions should not be limited to bedtime. 6/6 Delhi cannot control dengue one house at a time There is a limit to what one family can achieve if the surrounding neighbourhood continues to provide breeding sites. A mosquito breeding in a neighbouring building, vacant plot, construction site or discarded tyre pile does not respect property boundaries. That is why dengue prevention works best when households, housing societies, schools, workplaces and civic authorities act together. Dr Singh stressed that community participation is equally important because mosquito breeding in one home can affect an entire neighbourhood. He pointed to coordinated public-health action, civic surveillance, responsible behaviour and prompt medical attention as important ways of preventing seasonal transmission from becoming a wider public-health problem. That approach also reflects WHO's emphasis on surveillance, vector control and community engagement as connected parts of dengue prevention. For Delhi, the most useful question this monsoon may not be how many dengue cases have already been reported. It is whether the city can remove enough breeding opportunities before mosquito numbers rise further. A few minutes spent checking a cooler, flowerpot tray or rooftop container may seem insignificant. During dengue season, those small actions are exactly where prevention begins. Medical experts consulted This article includes expert inputs shared with TOI Health by: Dr Prakhar Singh, Co-Founder OSVI Healthcare & Advisor Mediways Health Foundation. Dr Inder Mohan Chugh, Vice Chairman & HOD – Pulmonology, Yatharth Super Speciality Hospital, Model Town, New Delhi. Inputs were used to explain how Delhi’s monsoon conditions can increase dengue risk, why early symptoms should not be ignored, and how timely medical care and mosquito-control measures can help prevent severe illness.
