B routine priorityPublic-health news

Swara Bhasker hospitalised with dengue: Cases on the rise; doctors explain warning signs, prevention and 7 ... - Bhaskar English

News Brief
Indian media report rising dengue cases during the monsoon season, highlighting celebrity Swara Bhasker’s hospitalization and expert advice on warning signs and prevention. Health authorities flag 23 districts in Uttar Pradesh, 15 in Maharashtra, and 13 in West Bengal as high-risk areas amid global records of over 1.46 crore dengue cases in 2024. Experts warn that stagnant water from rains and warm temperatures fuel mosquito breeding, while the dengue vaccine Qdenga, recently approved in India, complements but does not replace vector-control measures. Guidance stresses avoiding self-diagnosis, seeking medical evaluation for severe symptoms, and maintaining hydration and nutrition.
Original source text (English)
Get the latest news from your city Download Swara Bhasker hospitalised with dengue:Cases on the rise; doctors explain warning signs, prevention and 7 myths related to this disease 2 days agoAuthor: Shaily Acharya Loading advertisement... To Read Bhaskar English... Better News ExperienceRead in App ChromeContinue Bollywood actress Swara Bhasker has been hospitalised after being diagnosed with dengue. The actor shared a health update on Instagram Stories, posting a picture of herself in a hospital gown with an IV drip attached to her hand. Loading advertisement... Sharing the picture, she wrote, “Well that escalated fast. Dengue-fied and hospitalised!” Swara is not the only celebrity to have recently battled dengue. Balika Vadhu actress Avika Gor was also recently hospitalised with the infection. Several cities and districts across India are reporting cases of dengue and other mosquito-borne diseases during the monsoon. Loading advertisement... Jaipur is also among the places where health authorities have raised concerns about the risk of dengue and malaria outbreaks. According to the latest risk assessment, Uttar Pradesh has the highest number of districts identified as being at risk, with 23 districts. According to the latest risk assessment, Uttar Pradesh has the highest number of districts identified as being at risk, with 23 districts. Maharashtra follows with 15 districts, while 13 districts in West Bengal have also been flagged. Therefore, it is important to raise awareness about this disease. Dr. Rohit Sharma, Consultant - Internal Medicine, Apollo Spectra Hospital, Jaipur has spoken to Bhaskar English and explained- The warning signs and causes of dengue How to prevent dengue and what precautions to be taken? What should you do if you develop dengue symptoms? What is dengue and how does it spread? Dengue is the fastest-spreading mosquito-borne disease in the world. According to the 'World Health Organization' (WHO), more than 1.46 crore cases of dengue were reported worldwide in 2024. These are the highest cases ever recorded in a single year. Dengue is a viral infection spread by the bite of an infected mosquito, mainly the Aedes aegypti species. Why does dengue spread during monsoon? Aedes aegypti mosquitoes breed in stagnant water. During the rainy season, water can collect in flowerpot trays, coolers, buckets, tyres, drains, rooftops and other containers around homes. However, dengue is no longer considered only a monsoon disease. Warmer temperatures can allow mosquitoes to survive and reproduce for longer periods, while heavy rainfall as well as prolonged dry spells can create stagnant water and increase breeding opportunities. Why monsoon increases dengue risk? Rain creates stagnant-water breeding sites. Warm and humid conditions favour mosquito breeding. Water can collect in coolers, tyres, pots, buckets, rooftops and other containers. People may spend more time indoors, but mosquitoes can still bite inside homes. Warning signs of dengue Dengue symptoms usually appear after an infected mosquito bites a person. Common symptoms include: Importantly, serious complications can sometimes develop when the fever starts coming down. Dr. Rohit says that therefore, improvement in temperature does not always mean the danger has passed. Anyone developing warning signs should seek immediate medical evaluation rather than relying on home treatment. Dengue vs malaria: What is the difference? Dengue and malaria are both mosquito-borne diseases, but they are caused by different organisms. Dengue: Caused by the dengue virus and mainly spread by infected Aedes mosquitoes. Malaria: Caused by Plasmodium parasites and spread by infected Anopheles mosquitoes. Both can cause fever, chills and weakness, which is why testing may be needed to determine the cause. What preventive measures should be taken? Dengue prevention primarily involves preventing mosquito breeding and avoiding mosquito bites. Since Aedes mosquitoes can breed in small amounts of stagnant water, households should regularly empty and clean coolers, buckets, flowerpots, tyres and other water-holding containers. What should you do if you develop dengue symptoms? If you develop a high fever along with severe body pain, headache, vomiting or rash during the dengue season, consult a doctor. Do not self-diagnose based only on symptoms. Your doctor may recommend blood tests depending on how long you have had symptoms and your clinical condition. What precautions should be taken while eating food? During dengue, adequate hydration and nutrition are important because fever, sweating and vomiting can increase the risk of dehydration. Patients should consume- Clean drinking water and freshly prepared, hygienically handled food. Easily digestible meals such as rice, khichdi, soups, vegetables and fruits can be included according to tolerance. There is no specific food that cures dengue. Patients should avoid unhygienic, raw or improperly stored foods that could cause additional infection. Smaller, frequent meals may be easier to tolerate when appetite is reduced. What about dengue vaccines in India? India has also taken steps towards making dengue vaccination available. The dengue vaccine Qdenga, developed by Takeda Pharmaceutical, has received regulatory approval in India. The vaccine is a tetravalent live-attenuated vaccine designed to provide protection against all four dengue virus serotypes. It is administered in two doses. However, vaccination does not replace basic mosquito-control measures. People should continue to prevent stagnant water and protect themselves from mosquito bites. Are home remedies for dengue effective? Dr. Rohit says “Home remedies such as coconut water, fruit juices, herbal preparations and papaya leaf products are commonly used during dengue. While adequate fluids and nutritious food can support recovery, no home remedy has been proven to cure dengue or reliably prevent severe complications.” “Papaya leaf preparations, in particular, should not replace medical treatment or monitoring. Patients should follow their doctor's advice regarding hydration, testing and medicines,” he added futher. Seven Myths of Dengue Myth 1: Papaya leaf juice cures dengue.Fact: There is insufficient evidence that it cures dengue. Myth 2: Dengue always causes dangerously low platelets.Fact: Platelet counts vary, and overall clinical condition matters. Myth 3: Once fever disappears, the patient is safe.Fact: Warning signs can appear as fever subsides. Myth 4: Antibiotics cure dengue. Fact: Dengue is viral, so antibiotics do not treat it. Myth 5: Dengue spreads directly between people.Fact: It is primarily transmitted through infected Aedes mosquitoes. Myth 6: Only children develop severe dengue.Fact: Adults can also become seriously ill. Myth 7: Every fever is dengue.Fact: Diagnosis requires clinical assessment and, when appropriate, testing. . 坦帕湾蚊子激增,皮尼拉斯县确认两例登革热病例 - Hoodline

Pinellas County Confirms Two Dengue Cases as Tampa Bay Mosquito Surge Widens - Hoodline
新闻简报佛罗里达州皮内拉斯县确认了两例本地感染的 登革热 病例,这是坦帕湾地区更广泛的蚊媒疾病激增的一部分。暴发 与夏季暴雨有关,导致蚊子数量急剧增加,特别是埃及伊蚊和白纹伊蚊,它们通过叮咬传播登革热。这些蚊子在白天很活跃,增加了晚上之后的暴露风险。佛罗里达州卫生部和皮尼拉斯县蚊虫控制中心正在协调 监测 和预防工作。皮尼拉斯县蚊虫控制中心于 2026 年 8 月 7 日发布了先前的警告,强调了在病例确诊之前威胁的加剧。邻近地区希尔斯伯勒县继 7 月底出现首例登革热病例后,已于 2026 年 8 月 5 日报告了夏季第二例登革热病例。蚊子活动激增促使多轮杀虫剂治疗和有针对性的地面检查。然而,受影响的社区、患者关系和登革热血清型等具体细节仍在调查中。登革热不会直接在人与人之间传播,但需要受感染的蚊子来传播病毒。这种情况反映了佛罗里达州反复出现的问题,该州近年来在登革热活动方面领先美国,2024 年有 91 例本地感染病例,2025 年有 62 例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 8 月 11 日。事件日期没有明确说明,但案件确认发生在本周,并于 2026 年 8 月 7 日发出了事先警告。
地点与对象
佛罗里达州皮内拉斯县,重点关注坦帕湾。受影响人群包括接触蚊媒 登革病毒 的居民。
事件
皮内拉斯县卫生官员确认了两例本地感染的 登革热 病例。这些病例是坦帕湾更广泛的蚊媒疾病激增的一部分,与夏季暴雨和蚊子数量增加有关。登革热是由埃及伊蚊和白纹伊蚊传播的病毒感染。
规模、影响与风险
皮尼拉斯县确诊两例登革热病例。7 月中旬,希尔斯伯勒县蚊子数量激增,诱捕器数量从 142 只增加到 18,000 只。由于伊蚊白天的叮咬行为,登革热 传播 会增加日常户外活动期间的暴露风险,因此值得关注。佛罗里达州卫生部和当地蚊虫控制机构正在协调 监测 和预防措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
媒介 控制小组正在进行雾化、空中杀幼虫和地面检查,以减少蚊子数量。登革病毒 的确切社区、患者关系和血清型仍在调查中。病例的确认首先由《坦帕自由报》报道,并得到《坦帕湾时报》的证实。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pinellas County Confirms Two Dengue Cases as Tampa Bay Mosquito Surge Widens - Hoodline

News Brief
Pinellas County, Florida, confirmed two locally acquired dengue fever cases as part of a broader mosquito-borne illness surge in the Tampa Bay area. The outbreak is linked to heavy summer rainstorms that triggered a dramatic increase in mosquito populations, particularly Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus, which transmit dengue through bites. These mosquitoes are active during daylight hours, raising exposure risks beyond evening hours. The Florida Department of Health and Pinellas County Mosquito Control are coordinating surveillance and prevention efforts. Prior warnings were issued on August 7, 2026, by Pinellas County Mosquito Control, highlighting the elevated threat before the cases were confirmed. Hillsborough County, a neighboring area, had already reported its second dengue case of the summer on August 5, 2026, following an initial case in late July. The surge in mosquito activity has prompted multiple rounds of insecticide treatments and targeted ground inspections. However, specific details such as the affected neighborhoods, patient connections, and the dengue serotype remain under investigation. Dengue does not spread directly between humans but requires an infected mosquito to transmit the virus. This situation reflects a recurring issue in Florida, which has led the U.S. in dengue activity in recent years, with 91 locally acquired cases in 2024 and 62 in 2025.

Subscribe Daly City 64°F +59 +59 +59 +57 +58 +58 +58 +58 +59 +60 +62 +63 +64 +64 +64 +64 +62 +62 +60 +59 +58 +58 +58 +58 Wed 64°F 57°F +58 +58 +59 +59 +59 +59 +59 +58 +59 +60 +60 +62 +62 +63 +63 +62 +62 +61 +60 +59 +59 +59 +58 +58 Thu 63°F 58°F +58 +58 +59 +59 +59 +59 +59 +59 +60 +61 +62 +63 +63 +64 +63 +63 +63 +62 +61 +61 +60 +60 +59 +59 Fri 64°F 58°F +59 +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +59 +59 +60 +61 +62 +63 +63 +63 +63 +63 +63 +63 +62 +61 +60 +60 +59 +58 Sat 63°F 58°F +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +59 +59 +60 +60 +61 +61 +61 +61 +61 +61 +61 +61 +60 +60 +59 +59 +58 Sun 61°F 58°F +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +58 +59 +59 +59 +59 +59 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +59 +59 +59 +59 +59 Mon 60°F 58°F +59 +59 +59 +59 +58 +58 +59 +59 +59 +59 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +59 +59 +59 Tue 60°F 58°F +59 +59 +59 +58 +58 +58 +59 +59 +59 +60 +60 +60 +61 +61 +62 +62 +63 +63 +62 +62 +62 +61 +61 +60 +60 +60 +60 +59 +59 +59 +60 +60 +61 +62 +62 +63 +63 +63 +63 +63 +63 +62 +62 +62 +61 +61 +61 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +60 +61 +62 +62 +63 +64 +64 +64 +64 +64 +64 +64 +64 +64 +64 +62 +63 +63 +62 More forecasts: Atlanta 30 day forecast Tampa/ Crime & Emergencies Pinellas County Confirms Two Dengue Cases as Tampa Bay Mosquito Surge Widens By James Rodriguez Published on August 11, 2026 Source: Unsplash/ National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases Pinellas County health officials confirmed two locally acquired cases of dengue fever this week, adding the county to a growing list of Tampa Bay communities grappling with mosquito-borne illness after a summer of heavy rain. The cases mark a new front in a season that has already stretched vector control teams across the region, with fogging trucks, aerial larviciding, and ground inspections underway to knock down mosquito populations before more people get sick. The confirmation, first reported by the Tampa Free Press, comes just days after neighboring Hillsborough County confirmed its own second locally acquired dengue case of the summer on August 5, following an initial case in late July, according to the Tampa Bay Times. That report noted the surge is tied to a dramatic jump in mosquito populations, with trap collections in Hillsborough County rocketing from 142 mosquitoes in a single trap in June to roughly 18,000 mosquitoes in mid-July following summer rainstorms. Hillsborough officials responded with multiple rounds of aerial and truck insecticide treatments, the newspaper reported. The Florida Department of Health in Pinellas County said it is coordinating dengue surveillance and prevention measures with Pinellas County Mosquito Control, which participates in the broader effort to track and contain the virus locally. In response to the new cases, vector control teams increased truck fogging, conducted aerial larviciding, and carried out targeted ground inspections to find and eliminate potential mosquito breeding sites. Warning Signs Came Before the Diagnoses Pinellas County Mosquito Control had already flagged rising risk days earlier. On August 7, the agency issued a public advisory warning that mosquito-borne virus transmission had increased locally following heavy summer rains, per Pinellas County Government. That warning arrived before the two human dengue cases were confirmed, underscoring how vector control officials detected the elevated threat ahead of the diagnoses. The timing fits a pattern playing out statewide. Before Pinellas County's cases were confirmed, Florida health officials had already recorded at least three locally acquired dengue cases this summer in Hillsborough, Palm Beach, and Miami-Dade counties, according to WUSF. South Florida county health departments issued their own mosquito-borne illness alerts in early August, a trend Hoodline covered in its report on dengue alerts rattling Palm Beach and Miami-Dade. Why Rain Triggers a Mosquito Boom The biology behind the sudden spike traces back to the mosquitoes themselves. Female Aedes aegypti mosquitoes, the primary vector for dengue, lay eggs that can remain dormant in dry soil or containers for up to eight months until rain triggers hatching, WUSF reported, citing expert research on the species. That means a single heavy rain event can awaken a season's worth of dormant eggs almost simultaneously, feeding the kind of population explosion seen in Hillsborough's trap counts. Infected Aedes aegypti mosquitoes transmit dengue through their bites, and unlike many native mosquito species that feed mostly at dusk or overnight, Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus mosquitoes bite during daylight hours. That daytime activity means routine outdoor chores and errands carry real exposure risk, not just evening hours in the yard. Florida's Recurring Dengue Problem Pinellas County's cases land within a state that has repeatedly led the continental United States in dengue activity. Florida recorded 91 locally acquired cases across 10 counties in 2024, along with more than 1,000 travel-associated infections, with Miami-Dade County leading the state that year at 50 local cases, according to Outbreak News Today. In 2025, the state recorded 62 locally acquired cases, but the geography shifted: Brevard County accounted for more than half of them with 35 cases, while Miami-Dade recorded 25, marking the first time a Central Florida county led the state in local transmission. Pinellas County Mosquito Control monitors disease risk across all 24 local municipalities using 58 designated mosquito trap sites and eight sentinel chicken coops, which are tested weekly by state health labs, according to Pinellas County Government. The sentinel chickens serve as an early warning system, flagging when viruses begin circulating in wild bird populations before they spread more widely. That surveillance network sits alongside newer technology already deployed next door — in 2025, Hillsborough County Mosquito Management partnered with the University of South Florida to deploy AI-enabled smart traps and drone mapping aimed at identifying breeding hot spots like discarded tires, a program Hoodline detailed last year. What Dengue Looks Like and How to Avoid It Dengue is a viral illness, and symptoms typically develop within 15 days of an infected mosquito bite. Health authorities note the illness generally does not cause respiratory symptoms, but it can bring high fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and sometimes a rash. Dengue does not spread directly from person to person — it requires an infected mosquito to bite and transmit the virus. Health authorities are urging residents to eliminate standing water around their homes, including in buckets, flowerpots, pool covers, and trash cans, since these are common breeding sites for Aedes aegypti. Officials also recommend fixing broken screens on doors and windows, wearing loose long-sleeved clothing outdoors, and applying EPA-registered insect repellents to skin and clothing. Residents who suspect dengue symptoms are advised to contact a healthcare provider. DOH-Pinellas maintains ongoing surveillance for vector-borne pathogens across Florida, which also includes tracking for West Nile virus, chikungunya, malaria, St. Louis encephalitis, and Eastern equine encephalitis. It remains unclear which specific neighborhoods within Pinellas County the two dengue cases originated from, whether the two patients are connected in any way, or which serotype of the dengue virus was identified — questions that remain open as investigators continue their work. 棕榈滩县确诊 登革热 病例。如何保持安全 - AOL.com

Case of dengue fever confirmed in Palm Beach County. How to stay safe - AOL.com
新闻简报佛罗里达州卫生官员于 2026 年 8 月 3 日确认棕榈滩县出现一例本地感染的 登革热 病例,这是该县今年首次报告此类病例。佛罗里达州卫生部和棕榈滩县蚊虫控制中心联合启动了 监测 和 媒介 控制措施,包括有针对性地使用杀虫剂以及消除蚊子滋生地的公共咨询。虽然登革热很少致命,但症状可能很严重,从高烧和关节疼痛到罕见但危及生命的出血并发症。卫生当局强调通过使用驱虫剂、经过氯菊酯处理的衣服和纱窗进行预防,并指出目前没有国家被疾病预防控制中心列为登革热高发国家。此案发生之前,邻近的迈阿密戴德县也同时发布了一份报告,突显了该地区持续保持警惕。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-11;八月事件报道
地点与对象
美国佛罗里达州棕榈滩县(N1、N2、N3)。
事件
佛罗里达州棕榈滩县(N1、N3)卫生部报告了一例 登革热 确诊病例。该报告已得到确认,而非历史或指导(N1、N3)。
规模、影响与风险
棕榈滩县确诊一例本地病例(N1、N2、N3)。迈阿密戴德县当天报告了当地病例(N4)。登革热 很少致命,但可能会导致疼痛和衰弱 (N18)。症状包括发烧、头痛、眼痛、肌肉和关节疼痛、出血、呕吐和腹泻,通常持续 4-7 天(N19、N20、N21、N22)。重症登革热(登革出血热)很少见,但如果不治疗可能致命(N23、N24)。
应对、状态与不确定性
佛罗里达州棕榈滩县卫生部和棕榈滩县蚊虫控制中心正在协调 监测 和预防工作 (N5)。预防措施包括使用驱虫剂、用氯菊酯处理衣物、清除积水以及保持纱窗完好无损(N26、N27、N28)。目前没有国家属于 CDC 登革热 (N11) 的“高 传播”类别。截至 7 月 1 日,没有任何国家报告前三个月内旅行者出现 10 例或以上病例 (N12)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Case of dengue fever confirmed in Palm Beach County. How to stay safe - AOL.com

News Brief
Florida health officials confirmed a single locally acquired dengue fever case in Palm Beach County on August 3, 2026, marking the first such report in the county this year. The Florida Department of Health and Palm Beach County Mosquito Control launched joint surveillance and vector-control measures, including targeted insecticide application and public advisories on eliminating mosquito breeding sites. While dengue is rarely fatal, symptoms can be severe, ranging from high fever and joint pain to rare but life-threatening hemorrhagic complications. Health authorities emphasized prevention through repellent use, permethrin-treated clothing, and window screens, noting that no countries are currently classified by the CDC as experiencing high dengue transmission. The case follows a simultaneous report in neighboring Miami-Dade County, underscoring ongoing regional vigilance.
Original source text (English)
Case of dengue fever confirmed in Palm Beach County. How to stay safe Someone has recently contracted dengue fever in Palm Beach County. The Florida Department of Health in Palm Beach County sent an advisory about a confirmed case of the mosquito-borne illness on Aug. 3. Miami-Dade County reported a local case on the same day. “DOH-Palm Beach and Palm Beach County Mosquito Control are coordinating surveillance and prevention efforts,” reads the Florida Department of Health in Palm Beach County release. Dengue fever is a disease related to the viruses that cause West Nile virus and yellow fever. Several cases are reported in Florida each year from people traveling to areas that have the disease, which usually include the Caribbean, Central and South America and Asia, according to the Florida Department of Health. Cyclospora in Palm Beach County: Cyclospora cases rise in Palm Beach County. What to know on outbreak It is common in many countries around the world and almost half of the world’s population live in areas at risk of dengue, according to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control. Still, no countries are in the CDC’s “high transmission” category for dengue fever right now. No countries have had 10 or more cases reported among travelers in the last three months as of July 1. How is dengue fever transmitted? Dengue infection is acquired through the bite of a certain species of mosquito. These include Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus. Both types of mosquito live in Florida. What are the symptoms of dengue fever? Dengue fever can be painful and debilitating, but it is rarely fatal, according to the Florida Department of Health. Symptoms start between three and 14 days after an infected mosquito bite. They include a sudden onset of fever, severe headache, eye pain, muscle and joint pain and bleeding. Vomiting and diarrhea are also possible. Symptoms usually last four to seven days. A more severe form of dengue is dengue hemorrhagic fever. It is rare, but it can be fatal if not treated. There is no treatment for dengue fever, but symptoms and complications can be managed. How to avoid dengue fever? People can protect themselves from getting bitten by mosquitoes. They can apply insect repellent to exposed skin or clothing, treat clothing with products containing 0.5% permethrin, be careful not to leave out standing water (where mosquitoes breed) and keep screens on windows. Maya Washburn is a Trending News Reporter for The Palm Beach Post, part of the USA TODAY Florida-Network. Reach her at mwashburn@pbpost.com. Support local journalism: Subscribe today. One confirmed local case in Palm Beach County (N1, N2, N3). Miami-Dade County reported a local case on the same day (N4). Dengue fever is rarely fatal but can be painful and debilitating (N18). Symptoms include fever, headache, eye pain, muscle and joint pain, bleeding, vomiting, and diarrhea, typically lasting 4–7 days (N19, N20, N21, N22). Severe dengue (dengue hemorrhagic fever) is rare but can be fatal if untreated (N23, N24).
Response, status and uncertainty
The Florida Department of Health in Palm Beach County and Palm Beach County Mosquito Control are coordinating surveillance and prevention efforts (N5). Prevention measures include applying insect repellent, treating clothing with permethrin, eliminating standing water, and keeping window screens intact (N26, N27, N28). No countries are currently in the CDC’s 'high transmission' category for dengue (N11). As of July 1, no countries reported 10 or more cases among travelers in the prior three months (N12).

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

研究发现,随着多种病毒株一起传播,印度的登革热负担变得更加复杂

India’s dengue burden grows more complex as multiple virus strains circulate together, study finds
新闻简报印度ICMR-VRDL网络开展的一项为期两年的全国性研究记录了监测(登革热)四种血清型(DENV-1至DENV-4)在多个邦同时流行的情况,其中DENV-2占主导地位。近1/14的患者同时感染一种以上血清型,这种模式与重症登革热风险增加(包括出血并发症和危险的血小板计数过低)相关。研究人员警告称,这种高度地方性流行模式使登革热防控复杂化,并为未来的疫苗接种计划带来挑战,因疫苗效果随血清型而异,且优势株变化可改变疫苗接种动态。该研究呼吁在地方性流行地区持续开展血清型监测,并采取针对性疫苗接种策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于 2026-08-07 10:54 IST;研究期2023-2025年。
地点与对象
印度;全国 监测 覆盖 25 个州和联邦直辖区。
事件
ICMR-VRDL 网络进行的一项为期两年的全国性 监测 研究报告称,所有四种 监测 血清型(DENV-1 至 DENV-4)在印度同时流行,有证据表明存在并发感染和严重疾病风险增加。
规模、影响与风险
分析了 6,889 个实验室确认的登革热样本;DENV-2 占感染总数的一半以上;8 个州/联邦直辖区报告了所有四种血清型,15 个州/联邦直辖区同时报告了三种血清型;~7%的患者并发感染;并发感染与严重症状相关,包括血小板计数低、出血并发症和严重关节疼痛。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究强调需要连续血清型 监测 以及将登革热 疫苗 纳入公共卫生计划,特别是在流行地区;疫苗 的有效性因菌株和血清型变化而异,可能会影响 暴发 强度和 疫苗 性能;研究结果需要通过长期临床研究来证实。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India’s dengue burden grows more complex as multiple virus strains circulate together, study finds

News Brief
A nationwide two-year surveillance study by India’s ICMR-VRDL network has documented the simultaneous circulation of all four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1 to DENV-4) across multiple states, with DENV-2 predominating. Nearly one in 14 patients showed concurrent infections with more than one serotype, a pattern associated with higher risks of severe disease including bleeding complications and dangerously low platelet counts. Researchers warn this hyper-endemic pattern complicates dengue control and poses challenges for future vaccination programs, as vaccine effectiveness varies by serotype and shifts in dominant strains can alter outbreak dynamics. The study calls for sustained serotype monitoring and strategic vaccine deployment in endemic regions.

India World Sport Data Health Opinion Science Entertainment Premium Videos Podcast Photos Visual Stories Specials TH Explains eBooks TH Games Newsletter Lit For Life The Huddle Home Sci-Tech Health India’s dengue burden grows more complex as multiple virus strains circulate together, study finds The two-year nationwide surveillance study by the ICMR-VRDL network reveals increased risk of severe disease, and fresh challenges for the rollout of future dengue vaccination programmes Updated - August 07, 2026 10:54 pm IST - NEW DELHI Bindu Shajan Perappadan READ LATER SEE ALL Remove There are four dengue serotypes (DENV-1 to 4) and severity depends more on secondary infection than serotype, although DENV-2 has often been linked to severe outbreaks. File image for representation only | Photo Credit: Sandeep Saxena India may be entering a more perilous phase in its dengue epidemic, with multiple strains of the virus now circulating simultaneously across much of the country, raising concerns over an increased risk of severe disease, and posing fresh challenges for the rollout of future dengue vaccination programmes. A two-year nationwide surveillance study by the Indian Council of Medical Research’s (ICMR) Virus Research and Diagnostic Laboratory (VRDL) network found that all four dengue virus serotypes are circulating together in several parts of the country, with nearly one in 14 patients infected with more than one serotype at the same time. India’s first dengue vaccine arrives with a crucial safety caveat The study, titled ‘Pan-India Molecular Surveillance of Dengue Serotypes, 2023–2025’, analysed 6,889 laboratory-confirmed dengue samples from 45 laboratories across 25 States and Union Territories and found that DENV-2 remained the predominant serotype, accounting for over half of all infections. However, researchers said the broader concern was the widespread co-circulation of multiple serotypes, indicating dengue hyper-endemicity. Eight States and Union Territories reported the presence of all four serotypes, while 15 reported three circulating simultaneously. Around 7% of patients were infected with two or more dengue serotypes concurrently. QDENGA: Can the new Japanese vaccine end the dengue menace in India? | In Focus Podcast The study also found that patients with concurrent infections were more likely to develop severe manifestations of dengue, including dangerously low platelet counts, bleeding complications and severe joint pain, compared with those infected with a single serotype. While the findings need confirmation through long-term clinical studies, they suggest that simultaneous infection with multiple strains could worsen disease severity. There are four dengue serotypes (DENV-1 to 4) and severity depends more on secondary infection than serotype, although DENV-2 has often been linked to severe outbreaks. The emergence of hyperendemic transmission has implications beyond clinical care, the report notes. India currently has no dengue vaccine in its Universal Immunisation Programme, although indigenous vaccine candidates are in advanced stages of clinical development, and an internationally approved vaccine recently received regulatory clearance for import and sale in India. Researchers look at biomarkers which can predict dengue severity Continuous surveillance of circulating serotypes would be critical because vaccine effectiveness varies against different dengue strains, and shifts in the dominant serotype could influence both outbreak intensity and vaccine performance, researchers said. “Apart from routine steps taken to prevent mosquito breeding, there is certainly a need to incorporate the dengue vaccine into public health programmes, initially targeting endemic areas,” Gautam Menon, Professor of Physics and Biology, Ashoka University, said, speaking about the need for continuous surveillance. “But we also need more regular studies that can alert us about the magnitude of the problem, identify hotspots, and provide early warnings on how serotypes are shifting. This should be an ongoing, regularly reported study,” he added. Event
A two-year nationwide surveillance study by the ICMR-VRDL network reported co-circulation of all four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1 to DENV-4) in India, with evidence of concurrent infections and increased risk of severe disease.
Scale, impact and risk

佛罗里达州登革热季节加剧,李县进入戒备状态 - WINK News

Lee County on alert as dengue season ramps up in Florida - WINK News
新闻简报佛罗里达州卫生当局处于高度戒备状态,截至 2026 年 8 月,该州报告了 55 例登革热病例,其中包括 4 例本地传播的感染病例——希尔斯伯勒县有 2 例,李县无例。虽然大多数病例与旅行有关,但当地 传播 的存在表明,随着蚊子季节高峰,这种风险虽然罕见,但日益增加。李县今年已记录了两起与旅行相关的病例,该县已加强 监测,在专门的实验室中测试被困的蚊子,并准备在需要时进行社区喷洒。公共卫生专家指出,重症登革热 可能致命,埃及伊蚊(“后院蚊子”)也可以携带基孔肯雅热和寨卡病毒,这强调了消除积水的重要性。研究人员强调,局部病例正在受到密切关注,但尚未构成“暴发”。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 8 月 11 日发布;活动期间在 2026 年 8 月登革热季节期间持续进行。
地点与对象
美国佛罗里达州李县;佛罗里达州全州。
事件
随着佛罗里达州报告新的登革热感染病例,公共卫生官员正在测试蚊子并监测当地传播的登革热病例;李县处于高度戒备状态。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 8 月,佛罗里达州报告了 55 例登革热病例,其中 4 例本地传播病例(其中 2 例在希尔斯伯勒县),51 例与旅行相关;李县报告了 2 起与旅行相关的病例。重症登革热 可能是致命的;埃及伊蚊正在接受登革热和其他黄病毒检测。
应对、状态与不确定性
李县蚊虫控制区对登革热和相关病毒进行蚊子诱捕和实验室检测;在确诊或疑似病例周围进行主动喷洒。官员们强调社区行动以消除积水。佛罗里达州没有重大 暴发 报道;研究人员监测局部病例,但表示目前风险较低。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Lee County on alert as dengue season ramps up in Florida - WINK News

News Brief
Florida health authorities are on heightened alert as the state reports 55 dengue cases through August 2026, including four locally transmitted infections—two in Hillsborough County and none in Lee County. While most cases are travel-related, the presence of local transmission signals a rare but growing risk as mosquito season peaks. Lee County, which has recorded two travel-associated cases this year, has stepped up surveillance by testing trapped mosquitoes in a dedicated lab and stands ready to conduct neighborhood spraying if needed. Public health experts note that severe dengue can be fatal and that Aedes aegypti—the ‘backyard mosquito’—can also carry chikungunya and Zika, underscoring the importance of eliminating standing water. Researchers stress that localized cases are being watched closely but do not yet constitute an outbreak.

Advertisement Lee County on alert as dengue season ramps up in Florida Mosquito season is underway, and with it comes the heightened risk of mosquito-borne diseases like dengue. Show more Show less 2:42 2:42 Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Print Copy article link Save Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Print Copy article link Save LEE COUNTY, Fla. (WINK)—Public health officials are testing mosquitoes in Lee County as the state continues to report new cases of dengue virus. Most people who get the virus don't develop symptoms, according to the World Health Organization. However, severe cases can be deadly. The Florida Department of Health reported 55 dengue cases across the state as of Aug. 1. The vast majority of those cases came from travel, the health department said. However, four cases were recorded locally. That means they came from mosquitoes in Florida, which health researchers said is rare. Hillsborough County reported two locally transmitted cases last week. Florida has dengue cases almost every year, said Professor Michael Teng, an infectious disease researcher at the University of South Florida. It's usually imported by people who travel overseas. "There are a lot of dengue virus endemic regions in the Caribbean and South America, so it's not uncommon to see in Florida," Teng said. "I think when we start seeing local transmission of the virus, then we start to worry a little bit because that's the way we can get an outbreak of the virus." Researchers are keeping an eye on the localized cases, Teng said, but they aren't a concern just yet. The only major Florida outbreak was between 2009 and 2010 on Key West, when 28 cases were reported. That doesn't stop local officials from keeping an eye out. "We literally are always on alert," said Jenifer McBride, communications director for the Lee County Mosquito Control District. When there is a confirmed or suspected case of dengue, the district will use specialized trucks to spray the neighborhood, McBride said. Lee County reported two cases of dengue so far this year, according to the Florida Department of Health. Both came from international travel. The district also said it proactively tests mosquitoes in a lab at its Buckingham headquarters. Mosquitoes are trapped and brought back to the lab for immediate results. The lab will grind up mosquitoes and run them through a test to see if they're carrying specific diseases. The district will often test Aedes aegypti mosquitoes - known as the backyard mosquito. That species can transmit other diseases, Teng said, including the chikungunya and Zika virus. "We rely on our community members really to make sure that they're dumping out the standing water in their backyard," McBride said. "That helps us tremendously." Location and population
Lee County, Florida, USA; statewide Florida.
Event
Public health officials are testing mosquitoes and monitoring locally transmitted dengue cases as Florida reports new dengue infections; Lee County is on heightened alert.
Scale, impact and risk
Florida reported 55 dengue cases as of August 2026, with 4 locally transmitted cases (including 2 in Hillsborough County) and 51 travel-associated; Lee County reported 2 travel-associated cases. Severe dengue can be deadly; Aedes aegypti mosquitoes are being tested for dengue and other flaviviruses.

Officials emphasize community action to eliminate standing water. No major Florida outbreak is reported; researchers monitor localized cases but state current risk is low.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

登革热是佛罗里达州8种蚊媒疾病之一。需警惕的症状 - 塔拉哈西民主报

Dengue one of 8 Florida mosquito-borne diseases. Symptoms to watch for - Tallahassee Democrat
新闻简报塔拉哈西民主党人于 2026 年 8 月 7 日发表的文章强调 登革病毒 是佛罗里达州八种蚊媒疾病之一。它概述了突然发烧、严重头痛、眼睛疼痛、肌肉和关节疼痛以及出血等症状,并指出登革热由于与其他病毒相似而经常被误诊。佛罗里达州卫生部正在监测这些疾病,并向希尔斯伯勒和迈阿密戴德县等多个县发布了建议和警报,这些县报告了登革热病例。7月份确诊了4例,但文章没有具体说明重症病例的数量。建议居民采取预防措施防止蚊虫叮咬,例如使用含有避蚊胺、派卡瑞丁或其他经批准成分的驱虫剂。文章还提到了其他蚊媒疾病,如西尼罗河病毒、寨卡病毒和疟疾,但重点关注登革热作为主要主题。证据中没有提供有关登革热的具体调查或测试细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-08-07。事件日期:提供的证据中未报告。
地点与对象
美国佛罗里达州。发布建议或警报的县:阿拉楚阿县、莱昂县、棕榈滩县、帕斯科县、希尔斯伯勒县、迈阿密戴德县。受影响人群:这些地区的普通民众。
事件
本文讨论了佛罗里达州八种蚊媒疾病之一的 登革病毒,包括症状和预防措施。佛罗里达州卫生部监测这些疾病,并向某些县发出建议和警报。该州确认登革热是一种蚊媒疾病。
规模、影响与风险
七月份报告了四例登革热病例。登革热 症状包括突然发烧、严重头痛、眼睛疼痛、肌肉和关节疼痛以及出血。由于与其他病毒的相似性,该疾病经常被误诊。登革热可导致登革出血热、登革休克综合征等严重并发症,不过文章没有具体说明重症病例的数量。
应对、状态与不确定性
建议居民采取预防措施,减少蚊虫叮咬。文章没有提及登革热的具体调查或测试细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue one of 8 Florida mosquito-borne diseases. Symptoms to watch for - Tallahassee Democrat

News Brief
The article from the Tallahassee Democrat, published on August 7, 2026, highlights dengue virus as one of eight mosquito-borne diseases in Florida. It outlines symptoms such as sudden fever, severe headache, eye pain, muscle and joint pain, and bleeding, noting that dengue is often misdiagnosed due to its similarity with other viruses. The Florida Department of Health monitors these illnesses and has issued advisories and alerts for several counties, including Hillsborough and Miami-Dade, where dengue cases were reported. Four cases were confirmed in July, but the article does not specify the number of severe cases. Residents are advised to take precautions against mosquito bites, such as using repellents with DEET, picaridin, or other approved ingredients. The article also mentions other mosquito-borne diseases like West Nile virus, Zika virus, and malaria, but focuses on dengue as the primary topic. No specific investigation or testing details are provided for dengue in the evidence.
Original source text (English)
ULTIMATE VOTER GUIDE to candidates, issues, debates News Sports Opinion Email Us Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals STATE Dengue one of 8 Florida mosquito-borne diseases. Symptoms to watch for Mosquito-borne illness advisories and alerts have been issued for some Florida counties Cheryl McCloud USA TODAY NETWORK - Florida Updated Aug. 7, 2026, 10:55 a.m. ET Florida's Department of Health monitors mosquito-borne illnesses in the state. Symptoms of eight mosquito-borne illnesses seen in Florida. Mosquito-borne illness advisories and alerts have been issued for some Florida counties. Is there anything more annoying than mosquitoes when you're trying to enjoy the outdoors in Florida? Hm, now that we think about it, yes: Mosquitoes slipping into your home and buzzing you as you're trying to sleep! That's bad enough, but mosquitoes bring with them diseases, making them the world's deadliest animal. ➤ Meet the mosquito, the world's deadliest animal Shop Amazon for mosquito repellents Florida's Department of Health monitors mosquito-borne illnesses in the state, and there are several of them. Four cases of dengue were reported in the state in July. Here's what you should know about mosquito-borne illnesses in Florida, symptoms to watch for and how best to prevent mosquito bites. How many types of mosquito-borne illnesses are there in Florida? FDOH compiles weekly data on the following mosquito-borne viruses in a report called "Florida Arbovirus Surveillance": West Nile virus (WNV) Eastern equine encephalitis virus (EEEV) St. Louis encephalitis virus (SLEV) Dengue virus (DENV) Chikungunya virus (CHIKV) Zika virus (ZIKV) California encephalitis group viruses (CEV) Malaria Mosquito-borne illness alerts, advisories issued for these Florida counties A mosquito-borne illness advisory has been issued for the following counties: Alachua Leon Palm Beach Pasco A mosquito-borne illness alert has been issued for the following counties: Hillsborough Miami-Dade "A mosquito-borne illness advisory means the public should take precautions to reduce mosquito bites due to factors indicating local disease such as positive sentinels, very few presumptive or positive human cases, or other justifications," according to Sarasota County. "A mosquito-borne illness alert means that there has been heightened disease activity, a significant increase in vector (disease capable) mosquito populations, or an increase in locally-transmitted human cases. "Residents should continue to take precautions, but exercise additional caution in preventing mosquito bites." How many cases of mosquito-borne illnesses have been reported in Florida this year? According to the Florida Department of Health, as of Aug. 1, the following number of cases of mosquito-borne illnesses acquired in Florida have been reported: West Nile 1 in Alachua County 1 asymptomatic blood donor in Leon County Dengue 2 in Hillsborough County 1 in Miami-Dade County 1 in Palm Beach County What are symptoms of dengue fever? "Dengue fever can be a painful, debilitating disease but is rarely fatal," the Florida Department of Health said. Symptoms appear three to 14 days after the bite of an infected mosquito and include: sudden onset of fever severe headache eye pain muscle and joint pain (giving the disease the nickname “breakbone fever”) bleeding Gastrointestinal symptoms like vomiting and diarrhea may also be present in some cases. "Dengue fever symptoms usually lasts four to seven days. The disease is often diagnosed incorrectly because the symptoms are similar to influenza and other viruses." What are symptoms of West Nile virus? "West Nile virus is a mosquito-borne flavivirus that causes mild to severe illness," the Florida Department of Health said. "Approximately 80% of people who are infected with WNV will not show any symptoms at all." Mild symptoms include: fever headache body aches nausea vomiting Sometimes swollen lymph glands or a skin rash on the chest, stomach and back. Symptoms can last from a few days to several weeks. About one in 150 people infected with West Nile will develop severe illness. Symptoms include: high fever headache neck stiffness stupor disorientation coma tremors convulsions muscle weakness vision loss numbness paralysis "These symptoms may last several weeks, and neurological effects may be permanent. Severe illness can also result in death," FDOH said. What are symptoms of Eastern equine encephalitis virus? Most people infected with eastern equine encephalitis virus do not develop symptoms, according to the Florida Department of Health. For those who develop symptoms, the time from an infected mosquito bite to onset of illness ranges from four to 10 days. The CDC said symptoms of a mild illness include: fever chills body aches joint pain neurologic disease Symptoms of severe illness can include meningitis or encephalitis: fever headache vomiting diarrhea seizures behavioral changes drowsiness coma What are symptoms of St. Louis encephalitis virus? Most people infected with St. Louis encephalitis virus do not have symptoms, according to the Florida Department of Health. Symptoms of those who do become ill include: fever headache nausea vomiting tiredness What are symptoms of Chikungunya virus? Up to 28% of people who are infected with the Chikungunya virus will not have any symptoms, according to the Florida Department of Health. Symptoms of those who do can include: sudden high fever (usually higher than 102o F) which may be continuous or intermittent Severe joint pain that commonly involves the hands and feet Joint swelling Back pain Rash usually 2-5 days after fever starts Other symptoms may include headache, body ache, nausea, vomiting, and redness around the eyes. In unusual cases, infection can involve the brain, eyes, heart, kidney, and other organs "Fatal infections are rare, however many patients have chronic joint pain, arthritis, loss of energy, and depression lasting weeks to years," FDOH said. What are symptoms of Zika virus ? Many people infected with Zika virus will not have symptoms or will only have mild symptoms, according to the Florida Department of Health. Common symptoms of Zika include: fever rash headache joint pain conjunctivitis (red eyes) "Zika symptoms usually last for several days to a week," FDOH said. "People usually do not get sick enough to go to the hospital. For this reason, many people might not realize they have been infected." What are symptoms of California encephalitis group viruses? Most people infected with California serogroup viruses have no symptoms, according to the Mayo Clinic. Symptoms include: fever sore throat headache gastrointestinal symptoms What are symptoms of malaria? Symptoms of malaria depend on the type of parasite carried by the infected mosquito. In most cases, symptoms develop between seven and 30 days after the bite. Some symptoms may not appear up to a year later and, if not treated properly, can cause additional attacks, according to the Florida Department of Health. Common symptoms include such flu-like symptoms such as: fever chills sweats headache nausea vomiting body aches general malaise A bite by a mosquito carrying the Plasmodium falciparum parasite can cause severe and life-threatening disease. What are the best mosquito repellents to use? According to the Florida Department of Health, repellents with the following active ingredients provide the best protection: DEET Picaridin IR3535 Oil of lemon eucalyptus Para-menthane-diol 2-undecanone, a plant-derived ingredient Drain and cover helps keep mosquitoes away The FDOH offered this advice to help keep mosquitoes away from your Florida home: Drain Drain water from garbage cans, old tires, pool covers, or any other containers where water collects. Empty and clean birdbaths and pet water bowls at least once or twice a week. Protect boats and vehicles from rain with tarps that don’t accumulate water. Maintain swimming pools in good condition and ensure they are appropriately chlorinated. Empty plastic swimming pools when not in use. Cover If you must be outside when mosquitoes are active, cover up. Apply mosquito repellent to exposed skin and clothing. Use mosquito netting to protect children younger than 2 months old. 新闻简报据官员称,一种致命的登革热毒株,特别是2型,已被确定在德里流行。通过对登革热阳性样本进行 基因组 测序证实了这一点,但没有提供测试样本数量的详细信息。报告指出,今年迄今为止尚未有死亡记录,但已知 2 型菌株会引起更严重的症状,包括休克出血热。官员们加强了灭幼虫行动,并在脆弱地区增加了雾化措施,以阻止病毒的传播。MCD 在其运营的医院中预留了病床,并重点关注季风周期期间的幼虫消灭工作。然而,MCD 报告的病例数与卫生部长的数据之间存在差异,MCD 报告德里有 230 例病例,其他邦有 76 例。该报告还强调,老年人和儿童等免疫系统较弱的人特别容易感染该病毒。在 G20 峰会之前,MCD 已停止每周发布 媒介 传播疾病报告,这增加了当前局势的不确定性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Virulent dengue strain circulating in Delhi: Officials | Latest News Delhi - Hindustan Times

News Brief
A virulent dengue strain, specifically type-2, has been identified as circulating in Delhi, according to officials. This was confirmed through genome sequencing of dengue-positive samples, though details on the number of samples tested were not provided. The report notes that no deaths have been recorded so far this year, but the type-2 strain is known to cause more severe symptoms, including haemorrhagic fever with shock. Officials have intensified anti-larvae drives and increased fogging in vulnerable areas to combat the spread of the virus. The MCD has reserved hospital beds in its-run hospitals and is focusing on larval destruction during the monsoon cycle. However, there is a discrepancy in the reported case numbers between the MCD and the health minister’s data, with the MCD reporting 230 cases in Delhi and 76 from other states. The report also highlights that individuals with weak immune systems, such as the elderly and children, are particularly vulnerable to the virus. The MCD has stopped publishing weekly vector-borne disease reports ahead of the G20 summit, adding to the uncertainty in the current situation.
Original source text (English)
Virulent dengue strain circulating in Delhi: Officials Civic authorities said they have intensified anti-larvae drives and heightened vigilance for mosquito-borne diseases after a particularly virulent strain of dengue, type-2, has been found to be in circulation in the Capital Published on: Aug 12, 2026, 08:09:31 IST By Paras Singh Prefer HT on Google Share via Copy link Civic authorities said they have intensified anti-larvae drives and heightened vigilance for mosquito-borne diseases after a particularly virulent strain of dengue, type-2, has been found to be in circulation in the Capital. Virulent dengue strain circulating in Delhi: Officials A senior civic official said that genome sequencing of dengue-positive samples in Delhi has shown that the dengue virus type 2 (DENV-2) strain was found in a majority of the cases.Officials did not share details, including how many samples were sequenced and how many of them were type-2. No deaths, however, have been recorded so far this year. “Dengue has multiple strains. Type 1 can cause classic dengue fever along with chills and headaches while Type 2 can lead to haemorrhagic fever with shock and chills,” the official explained. Individuals with weak immune systems, such as the elderly, children and those with chronic illnesses, are particularly vulnerable. “We are still in the initial phase of the monsoon cycle and focusing on destruction at the larval stages,” the official added. Confirming this, mayor Parwesh Wahi said, “We have issued directions to increase the fogging in vulnerable areas and hospital beds have been reserved in MCD run hospitals. Anti-larvae drives are being intensified.” Five-year municipal data on detection of mosquito breeding shows that the weekly count of mosquito larvae detection peaks between 32nd and 38th week of the year. With incessant rain, the breeding rate has increased over the last fortnight. “The mosquito causing dengue breeds in clear water and waterlogged areas and the primary method of preventing the dengue is through breeding site reduction. With regular rain spells over the last 10 days, waterlogging has increased in various parts of the city,” the official quoted above added. MCD does not maintain data on strains. They come from tests carried out by AIIMS. MCD action taken report recorded 9,803 sites with larvae over the last week, around 12% of the overall positive sites (82,870) detected between January 1 and August 8 this year. According to MCD’s weekly vector-borne disease report dated August 8, at least 230 dengue cases have been reported so far in this year with an additional 76 cases where infection was acquired from other states. In 159 cases, the address of the patient was wrong or they could not be found. However, the figures do not match health minister Pankaj Kumar Singh’s report after a review last week. “So far, a total of 1,179 dengue cases have been reported in Delhi. From the NCR region, outside Delhi, around 210–212 cases have been reported,” he said. MCD officials did not respond to HT requests for comment on the discrepancy in the data. According to MCD data from previous years, 315 cases were reported in 2025, 322 in 2024, 244 cases in 2023, and 64 cases in 2022 during the corresponding period. The civic body had officially stopped making weekly vector-borne disease reports public ahead of the G20 summit in Delhi. Type 2 was in circulation at the time. Type 2 strain was also predominant during the city’s worst dengue outbreak in 2015 when 15,867 cases and 60 deaths were reported due to dengue over 12 months. Dengue is a viral infection transmitted to humans by the Aedes aegypti mosquito. While it is typically not fatal and most people do not develop symptoms, those who do typically experience high fever, headache, body aches, nausea, and rash. Most recover within 1-2 weeks, but it can be fatal in extreme cases. Delhi saw four confirmed dengue-related deaths in 2025, 11 in 2024, 19 in 2023, and nine cases in 2022. ABOUT THE AUTHOR Paras Singh Paras Singh is a New Delhi-based journalist with Hindustan Times, where he serves as Deputy Metro Editor. He reports on urban governance, the Delhi government, the Delhi BJP, water management, the environment, communities and city life. He joined Hindustan Times in 2021 after beginning his journalism career at The Times of India in 2016, where he covered civic bodies, the water and power sectors, and urban infrastructure. Over the last decade, some of his reporting has led to significant public impact, with investigations into environmental violations, corruption and illegal industrial activity contributing to multiple judicial interventions by the National Green Tribunal and the Delhi High Court. A graduate of St. Stephen's College, University of Delhi, Paras studied journalism from the Indian Institute of Mass Communication (IIMC), New Delhi. Having extensively covered the capital's evolving civic and governance landscape, his recent work increasingly explores Delhi's history, built heritage, urban evolution and the relationship between people and place, alongside continuing to report on governance and public policy. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

皮内拉斯县确认两例本地感染登革热病例

Two Locally Acquired Dengue Cases Confirmed In Pinellas County
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 11 日,佛罗里达州皮内拉斯县卫生官员确认了两例本地感染的 登革热 病例。佛罗里达州卫生部和皮尼拉斯县蚊虫控制中心正在协调 媒介 控制工作,包括加强雾化、空中杀幼虫和地面检查,以消除蚊子滋生地。当局敦促居民清除积水、修理窗纱、穿长袖衣服并使用 EPA 注册的驱虫剂,以降低 传播 风险。虽然登革热不会在人与人之间传播,但被蚊子叮咬后 15 天内可能会出现高烧、严重肌肉和关节疼痛以及皮疹等症状。建议有疑似症状的居民就医。报告中没有提供有关血清型或疾病严重程度的进一步细节。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Two Locally Acquired Dengue Cases Confirmed In Pinellas County

News Brief
On August 11, 2026, health officials in Pinellas County, Florida, confirmed two locally acquired cases of dengue fever. The Florida Department of Health and Pinellas County Mosquito Control are coordinating vector-control efforts, including intensified fogging, aerial larviciding, and ground inspections to eliminate mosquito breeding sites. Authorities urge residents to remove standing water, repair window screens, wear long sleeves, and apply EPA-registered repellents to reduce transmission risk. While dengue is not spread person-to-person, symptoms such as high fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and rash may develop within 15 days of a mosquito bite. Residents with suspected symptoms are advised to seek medical care. No further details on serotype or disease severity were provided in the report.
Original source text (English)
PINELLAS COUNTY, Fla. – Health officials in Pinellas County have confirmed two local cases of dengue fever, prompting response efforts to control the area’s mosquito population. The Florida Department of Health in Pinellas County announced it is coordinating surveillance and prevention measures alongside Pinellas County Mosquito Control. Vector control strategies currently underway include increased truck fogging, aerial larviciding, and targeted ground inspections to locate and eliminate potential breeding grounds. Dengue is a viral illness transmitted through the bite of an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito. It does not spread directly from person to person. Symptoms typically develop within 15 days of a bite and include high fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and occasionally a rash. Unlike many seasonal viruses, dengue generally does not cause respiratory symptoms. To reduce the risk of transmission, health authorities advise residents to eliminate standing water around homes by emptying items like buckets, flowerpots, pool covers, and trash cans. Additional recommendations include fixing broken screens on doors and windows, wearing loose long-sleeved clothing, and applying EPA-registered insect repellents to skin and clothing. DOH-Pinellas notes that state health agencies maintain ongoing surveillance for multiple vector-borne pathogens across Florida, including West Nile virus, Eastern equine encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, malaria, chikungunya, and dengue. Residents who suspect they are experiencing symptoms of dengue are advised to contact a healthcare provider. 蚊子警报:希尔斯伯勒县确认第八例当地登革热病例

Mosquito Alert: Eighth Local Dengue Case Confirmed In Hillsborough County
新闻简报佛罗里达州希尔斯伯勒县卫生官员于 2026 年 8 月 11 日确认了第八例本地感染的 监测 病例。希尔斯伯勒县佛罗里达州卫生部 (DOH-希尔斯伯勒) 报告了 诊断 病例,并正在与当地蚊虫控制小组合作,加强 监测 和预防工作。登革热由埃及伊蚊传播,感染后 15 天内会出现突然发烧、肌肉和关节严重疼痛、出现皮疹等症状。当局敦促居民清除积水、使用美国环保署注册的驱虫剂、穿防护服并修理纱窗以减少蚊子暴露。该县继续在全州范围内监测多种蚊媒病原体,包括西尼罗河病毒和基孔肯雅热,同时鼓励社区参与更广泛的疾病 监测。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-11;所提供的证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
美国佛罗里达州希尔斯伯勒县。
事件
佛罗里达州希尔斯伯勒县卫生部 (DOH-Hillsborough) 确认了第八例本地感染的人类 登革热 病例。该事件已得到确认并正在进行中,卫生当局正在协调 媒介 控制措施。
规模、影响与风险
希尔斯伯勒县报告了八起本地感染的登革热病例。登革热由埃及伊蚊传播,不会在人与人之间传染。症状包括突然发烧、严重的肌肉和关节疼痛以及皮疹,通常在被感染的叮咬后 15 天内出现。
应对、状态与不确定性
希尔斯伯勒卫生部正在与希尔斯伯勒县蚊子管理局协调部署 监测 和预防资源。建议居民排出积水、遮盖裸露的皮肤、使用 EPA 注册的驱虫剂并保护房屋安全。全州范围内继续监测蚊媒病原体,包括西尼罗河病毒、东部马脑炎、圣路易斯脑炎、疟疾、基孔肯雅热和登革热。鼓励居民报告更广泛的 监测 目击死鸟事件。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mosquito Alert: Eighth Local Dengue Case Confirmed In Hillsborough County

News Brief
Health officials in Hillsborough County, Florida, confirmed an eighth locally acquired case of dengue fever on August 11, 2026. The Florida Department of Health in Hillsborough County (DOH-Hillsborough) reported the diagnosis and is collaborating with local mosquito control teams to intensify surveillance and prevention efforts. Dengue is transmitted by Aedes aegypti mosquitoes and causes symptoms such as sudden fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and rash within 15 days of infection. Authorities urge residents to eliminate standing water, use EPA-registered repellents, wear protective clothing, and repair window screens to reduce mosquito exposure. The county continues statewide monitoring for multiple mosquito-borne pathogens, including West Nile virus and chikungunya, while encouraging community participation in broader disease surveillance.
Original source text (English)
HILLSBOROUGH COUNTY, Fla. – Health officials in Hillsborough County have confirmed an eighth human case of locally acquired dengue fever, keeping the region under an ongoing mosquito-borne illness alert. The Florida Department of Health in Hillsborough County (DOH-Hillsborough) announced the diagnosis and stated that it is coordinating with Hillsborough County Mosquito Management to deploy surveillance and prevention resources to curb local mosquito populations. Dengue fever is a viral infection transmitted through the bite of an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito. It is not contagious between humans. Symptoms typically develop within 15 days of an infected bite and can include a sudden fever, severe muscle and joint aches, and a rash. Unlike typical flu viruses, dengue generally does not cause respiratory symptoms. To reduce the risk of transmission, health officials advise residents to take preventative steps against mosquito bites and breeding: Drain standing water: Empty water from items such as tires, buckets, flowerpots, pool covers, and coolers. According to DOH-Hillsborough, a single water-filled tire can serve as a breeding ground for hundreds of mosquitoes. Cover exposed skin: Wear loose-fitting, long-sleeved shirts and long pants when outdoors. Use insect repellent: Apply EPA-registered repellents to skin and clothing as directed. EPA-registered repellents are tested and approved as safe for general use, including for pregnant and breastfeeding women. Secure homes: Repair damaged window, door, porch, and patio screens to prevent mosquitoes from entering indoor spaces. DOH-Hillsborough continues statewide monitoring for various mosquito-borne pathogens, including West Nile virus, Eastern equine encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, malaria, chikungunya, and dengue. Residents are also encouraged to report dead bird sightings through the Florida Fish and Wildlife Conservation Commission website to assist with broader disease surveillance across the state. Scale, impact and risk
Eight locally acquired dengue cases reported in Hillsborough County. Dengue is transmitted by Aedes aegypti mosquitoes and is not contagious between humans. Symptoms include sudden fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and rash, typically within 15 days of an infected bite.
Response, status and uncertainty
DOH-Hillsborough is coordinating with Hillsborough County Mosquito Management to deploy surveillance and prevention resources. 2 locally acquired dengue fever cases in Pinellas; county to boost mosquito control efforts

News Brief
Pinellas County, Florida, reported two locally acquired dengue fever cases on 11 August 2026, prompting stepped-up mosquito control operations. The Florida Department of Health confirmed the infections and is coordinating with Pinellas County Mosquito Control to reduce Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus populations through intensified fogging, aerial spraying, and breeding-site inspections, with priority in Palm Harbor and communities along Tampa Bay. Health authorities urge residents to remove standing water and use insect repellent to prevent further spread. No severe cases or deaths have been reported.

(WFLA) — Pinellas County is ramping up its mosquito removal efforts after the Florida Department of Health reported two locally acquired cases of dengue fever. The county health department announced the confirmed cases on Tuesday afternoon and said it is coordinating treatment plans with Pinellas County Mosquito Control. Residents may notice an increase in truck fogging, aerial spraying, and ground inspections of potential breeding locations as the county works to reduce mosquito populations. Advertisement Advertisement 20+ Border Collies removed from feces-covered home of former Florida breeder: sheriff The county said efforts will be concentrated in the Palm Harbor area, but residents across Pinellas are advised to take precautions. Truck fogging data from the county shows much of Palm Harbor was treated Tuesday morning. Parts of St. Petersburg, Clearwater, Safety Harbor and Oldsmar were also treated over the last few days, particularly areas along Tampa Bay. You can visit this site to see recent treatments in your area. Symptoms of dengue fever can include flu-like symptoms such as muscle aches, joint pain, fever, and sometimes a rash, DOH said. Advertisement Advertisement Mother of 2 kidnapped, killed by Pinellas County deputy who took his own life, sheriff says The disease is not contagious and is transmitted by bites from Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus mosquitoes, which are active during the day and breed in containers, the DOH said. Residents can help protect themselves by dumping out any standing water that may collect on their property. Additionally, dressing in light-colored, long-sleeved clothing and using insect repellent can help prevent bites. 登革热病例增加后美国发布肯尼亚旅行健康警报 - Nomad Lawyer

US Issues Kenya Travel Health Alert Following Rise in Dengue Cases - Nomad Lawyer
新闻简报美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 于 2026 年 8 月上旬发布了肯尼亚旅行健康通知,强调近期蒙巴萨和热门野生动物园等沿海地区 登革热 病例有所增加。虽然该建议并不建议不要前往该国,但它敦促国际旅行者在白天采取严格的防蚊措施,对登革热症状保持警惕,并在患病时立即就医。肯尼亚卫生官员正在积极监测情况,但报告中没有包括具体病例数,因此暴发的确切规模尚不清楚。鼓励旅客使用避蚊胺或派卡瑞丁驱虫剂、穿长袖衣服并呆在经过筛查的住宿场所以降低风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-08-07。如文章日期所示(2026 年 8 月 6 日),CDC 旅行健康通知于 2026 年 8 月上旬发布。
地点与对象
美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 向肯尼亚发布了旅行健康警报,特别强调蒙巴萨等沿海目的地和国际游客聚集的国家野生动物园的登革热活动不断增加。
事件
疾病预防控制中心发布了旅行健康通知,警告肯尼亚登革热风险增加。这篇文章报道了这一通知,并将其归咎于疾病预防控制中心,这是一份经过证实的建议,而不是一份推测性报告。
规模、影响与风险
该通报指出,肯尼亚沿海地区和野生动物园的登革热病例有所增加,这表明旅行者感染蚊媒感染的风险增加。所提供的证据中没有提供具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
疾病预防控制中心的建议建议旅行者在白天持续采取防蚊措施,识别早期登革热症状,并在出现症状时寻求医疗护理。不建议避免旅行,肯尼亚卫生当局将继续监测情况。文章没有提供详细的流行病学数据,因此 暴发 的确切大小仍不确定。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

US Issues Kenya Travel Health Alert Following Rise in Dengue Cases - Nomad Lawyer

News Brief
The U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) has issued a travel health notice for Kenya in early August 2026, highlighting a recent increase in dengue fever cases across coastal regions such as Mombasa and popular safari parks. While the advisory does not advise against visiting the country, it urges international travelers to adopt rigorous daytime mosquito protection, stay alert for dengue symptoms, and seek prompt medical attention if illness occurs. Kenyan health officials are actively monitoring the situation, but the report does not include specific case numbers, leaving the precise scale of the outbreak unclear. Travelers are encouraged to use DEET or picaridin repellents, wear long‐sleeved clothing, and stay in screened accommodations to mitigate risk.

Geographic Reference By Kunal K Choudhary August 06, 2026 4 min read Image generated by AI The US Centers for Disease Control has issued a travel health notice for Kenya due to rising cases of dengue fever, particularly in popular coastal destinations and national parks. Health Centers for Disease Control Issues Kenya Dengue Warning Health authorities have advised international visitors to take strict precautions against mosquito bites when traveling through East Africa's leading tourism hubs. According to travel advisories from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), dengue has emerged as a key travel-related risk in Kenya. The alert encourages travelers to maintain continuous mosquito protection, particularly because the Aedes mosquitoes that transmit the virus are active primarily during daylight hours. The health notice does not advise against visiting the country, but instead emphasizes prevention, early symptom recognition, and medical preparation. Outbreaks are linked to warmer temperatures, rising mosquito populations, and increased international passenger traffic. For travelers planning safaris or beach holidays, understanding the transmission environment is essential to preventing infection. High-Risk Areas and Environmental Transmission Vectors In Kenya, dengue activity has historically concentrated in regions where climate conditions support mosquito breeding. Coastal areas, including Mombasa and other destinations along the Indian Ocean, face higher risk profiles due to their warm, humid tropical environments. Health Conditions Urban centers also present challenges as Aedes mosquitoes breed in containers holding standing water near hotels, residential areas, and public parks. Travelers on outdoor walking tours, beach excursions, and safari drives are exposed to higher daytime bite risks. The Kenya Ministry of Health continues to monitor regional disease vectors to protect both local communities and international arrivals. Essential Health Advisory and Travel Insurance Safeguards For the affected traveler, the issuance of a travel health notice has direct implications for health preparation and trip protection: Travel Insurance Coverage: Standard travel insurance policies generally do not permit travelers to cancel a trip due to "fear of travel" related to a health advisory. To recover non-refundable booking costs for cancellations based on health alerts, travelers must purchase Cancel for Any Reason (CFAR) coverage, which is usually only available shortly after making initial trip deposits. Emergency Medical Care: If a traveler contracts dengue while in Kenya, standard travel medical insurance will cover diagnostic tests, emergency consultations, and hospitalization costs, provided the policy lacks exclusions for tropical infections. Symptom Awareness: Common symptoms include high fever, severe headaches, pain behind the eyes, joint and muscle pain, skin rash, and nausea. Returning travelers who develop these symptoms should seek immediate medical attention and disclose their travel history. Medication Warning: Travelers showing symptoms of dengue should avoid taking aspirin or ibuprofen, as these medications can increase bleeding risks associated with severe dengue. Paracetamol should be used instead. Impact on East Africa's Tourism Growth and Health Security The travel health notice arrives as Kenya continues to expand its position in the global safari and beach tourism sectors. Wildlife conservation reserves and luxury eco-lodges attract millions of visitors annually, making safety communication essential to maintaining visitor confidence. Zoos, Aquariums & Preserves Addressing mosquito-borne disease risks requires ongoing coordination between tourism operators, municipal health boards, and international agencies. Hotels and lodges in high-risk zones have implemented preventative spraying and screened accommodations to protect guests. Travelers who prepare appropriate insect repellent containing DEET or picaridin, pack protective long-sleeved clothing, and choose screened lodging can continue to safely explore the country's diverse wildlife parks. US Issues Health Alert for Kenya 2026: Rising Dengue Fever Cases Impact Global Tourists - Nomad Lawyer

News Brief
The United States has issued a travel health alert for Kenya in 2026 due to a surge in dengue fever cases, which are being highlighted as a significant health risk for international tourists. The alert, issued by the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), emphasizes that dengue transmission is prevalent in coastal regions, urban centers, and outdoor tourism hubs, including areas around Mombasa and game parks. The risk is attributed to the presence of Aedes mosquitoes, which are active during daylight hours, making travelers vulnerable throughout the day. Despite the alert, US officials have stated that travel to Kenya should not be avoided, but they urge visitors to take strict preventative measures, such as using insect repellents containing DEET or picaridin, wearing long-sleeved clothing, and avoiding stagnant water. The tourism sector remains fully operational, with no major attractions closed. The alert underscores the growing importance of health security in global travel planning, as travelers increasingly prioritize disease alerts and vaccination schedules alongside itinerary planning. The CDC recommends a multi-layered approach to mosquito prevention, including environmental awareness and strategic accommodation choices, to mitigate the risk of infection.

Health By Preeti Gunjan August 06, 2026 4 min read Image generated by AI General Reference The United States has issued a formal travel health alert for Kenya following a surge in dengue fever cases. While the country remains open for tourism, health officials are urging visitors to implement strict preventative measures to avoid mosquito-borne infections. US CDC Issues Warning Over Dengue Transmission in Kenya The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) has identified dengue fever as a significant travel-related health risk for those visiting Kenya. The alert emphasizes that mosquito-borne diseases are prevalent not only in the country's famous coastal resorts but also within major urban centers and expansive game parks. Unlike many other mosquito species that are primarily active at night, the Aedes mosquitoes responsible for transmitting dengue are active during daylight hours. This means travelers are at risk throughout the day, necessitating continuous protection regardless of the time. Despite the alert, US officials have clarified that travel to Kenya should not be avoided. The guidance is intended to ensure that international visitors—who may have lower natural immunity to tropical diseases—approach their trips with informed preparation and early awareness. Health Conditions High-Risk Zones and Transmission Factors Dengue fever is increasingly becoming a global health challenge, driven by rising global temperatures, rapid urban growth, and the fluidity of international travel. In Kenya, the risk is concentrated in environments where mosquito breeding is most favorable. Coastal Regions: Areas surrounding Mombasa and other Indian Ocean destinations are high-risk due to the warm, humid climate. Urban Centers: Cities face elevated risks as mosquitoes frequently breed in stagnant water found in containers near hotels, public spaces, and residential neighborhoods. Outdoor Tourism Hubs: Visitors engaging in walking tours, beach activities, and nature reserve explorations are more exposed to bites. Recognizing Dengue Fever: Symptoms and Medical Risks Dengue is a viral infection that can vary from mild discomfort to life-threatening complications. Because the symptoms often mimic other tropical illnesses, early medical intervention and a detailed travel history are vital for an accurate diagnosis. Tourist Destinations Common Symptoms Include: High fever and severe headaches Pain located behind the eyes Acute joint and muscle pain Skin rashes Nausea, vomiting, and general fatigue While most patients recover with rest and medical care, some develop "severe dengue." This critical stage can lead to internal bleeding, respiratory distress, and dangerously low blood pressure. Essential Prevention Strategies for 2026 Travelers To mitigate the risk of infection, the CDC recommends a multi-layered approach to protection. Relying on a single method is often insufficient in high-transmission zones. Travel & Transportation Chemical and Physical Barriers Travelers should use insect repellents containing approved active ingredients such as DEET or picaridin. Additionally, wearing long-sleeved shirts, long trousers, and closed-toe footwear is recommended to minimize exposed skin. Strategic Accommodation When booking hotels or lodges, prioritize facilities that offer: Air conditioning to keep mosquitoes out of living spaces. Screened windows and doors. Active mosquito prevention and pest control systems. Environmental Awareness Avoid spending extended time near standing water, such as garden ponds or drainage areas, which serve as primary breeding grounds for the Aedes mosquito. Health Impact on Kenya's Tourism Infrastructure Kenya continues to be a premier global destination, and the health alert has not led to the closure of any major attractions. The tourism sector remains fully operational, with luxury safari lodges and coastal resorts maintaining standard safety protocols. The current situation underscores a broader shift in global travel: the integration of health security into the planning process. Travelers are now prioritizing disease alerts and vaccination schedules as much as itinerary planning. Pack Specialized Gear: Ensure a sufficient supply of high-grade repellent and lightweight, long-sleeved clothing. Insurance Review: Verify that travel insurance covers medical evacuation and treatment for tropical diseases. Medical Consultation: Discuss potential vaccinations or preventative medications with a healthcare provider. Health preparation is now an essential component of a successful international itinerary. 随着病例持续上升,美国向前往肯尼亚部分地区的旅行者发出登革热警报 - Dawan Africa

US Issues Dengue Alert for Travellers Visiting Parts of Kenya as Cases Continue to Rise - Dawan Africa
新闻简报由于感染病例上升,美国已向前往肯尼亚部分地区的旅行者发出登革热警报,其中包括沿海地区以及东北部的瓦吉尔县和加里萨县。该警报由美国驻内罗毕大使馆和疾病预防控制中心于 2026 年 8 月 6 日发布,是全球登革热 监测 计划的一部分。肯尼亚是登革热病例增加的 11 个国家之一,疾病预防控制中心已将其置于一级咨询之下,敦促旅行者采取正常的预防措施,同时对风险保持警惕。暴发 据信始于 2025 年底,之前的 暴发 报道是在 1980 年代初、2013-2014 年和 2021 年。健康专家指出,登革热是由埃及伊蚊传播的,埃及伊蚊在温暖、潮湿的环境中繁殖。肯尼亚的应对措施包括加强 监测 和公众意识宣传活动,因为它尚未广泛实施 疫苗接种。该建议并不阻止旅行,但强调采取预防蚊虫叮咬措施的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
肯尼亚目前的暴发据信始于2025年底,美国于2026年8月6日发布登革热警报,活动日期为2026年8月12日。
地点与对象
该警报将肯尼亚沿海地区以及东北部的瓦吉尔县和加里萨县列为关注地区,美国驻内罗毕大使馆也参与了该警报。
事件
疾病预防控制中心证实,在报告的感染病例增加以及对回国旅行者病例的担忧之后,美国向前往肯尼亚部分地区的旅行者发出登革热警报。
规模、影响与风险
肯尼亚是最新全球咨询中包含的 11 个国家之一,美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 向沿海地区和东北部县发布了一级咨询,建议旅行者采取正常预防措施,同时对感染风险保持警惕。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生官员预计将在受影响地区加强监测和公众宣传活动,而肯尼亚尚未将广泛的登革热疫苗接种纳入其国家免疫规划。

US Issues Dengue Alert for Travellers Visiting Parts of Kenya as Cases Continue to Rise - Dawan Africa

News Brief
The United States has issued a dengue alert for travellers visiting parts of Kenya, including the coastal region and northeastern counties of Wajir and Garissa, following a rise in infections. The alert, issued on August 6, 2026, by the US Embassy in Nairobi and the CDC, is part of the global dengue surveillance programme. Kenya is among 11 countries with increased dengue cases, and the CDC has placed it under a Level One advisory, urging travellers to take normal precautions while being vigilant about the risk. The outbreak is believed to have started in late 2025, with previous outbreaks reported in the early 1980s, 2013–2014, and 2021. Health experts note that dengue is transmitted by Aedes aegypti mosquitoes, which thrive in warm, humid environments. Kenya's response includes intensified surveillance and public awareness campaigns, as it has not yet implemented widespread vaccination. The advisory does not discourage travel but emphasizes the need for mosquito bite prevention measures.
Original source text (English)
Wednesday, August 12, 2026 18°C•Cloudy8 km/hNairobi Notifications blocked LoginRegister Search HomePoliticsBusinessOpinionSportsExplainersCryptocurrencyCulture & SocietyFeaturesNewsTechPodcastsVideosBlockchain SomaliaKenyaDjiboutiEthiopiaEritrea SomaliaKenyaDjiboutiEthiopiaEritrea US Issues Dengue Alert for Travellers Visiting Parts of Kenya as Cases Continue to Rise August 6, 2026 3 min read By Benadeta Mwaura - Reporter Kenya, August 6, 2026 - The United States has issued a fresh health advisory warning travellers about the risk of dengue fever in parts of Kenya following a rise in infections, placing the country back on the list of destinations under the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention's (CDC) global dengue surveillance programme. The alert, issued through the US Embassy in Nairobi and the CDC, identifies Kenya's coastal region as well as the northeastern counties of Wajir and Garissa as areas of concern. Although the notice does not advise Americans against travelling to Kenya, it urges visitors to take precautions to protect themselves against mosquito bites. According to the CDC, Kenya remains under a Level One advisory category, meaning travellers are advised to practise normal precautions while remaining alert to the risk of infection. "Dengue is a mosquito-borne virus common in tropical and subtropical climates that can lead to fever, aches and pains, nausea and rashes," the agency said. The CDC further advised travellers to use insect repellents, wear long-sleeved clothing and sleep in screened or air-conditioned rooms to minimise the risk of exposure. Kenya is one of 11 countries included in the latest global advisory following an increase in reported infections and concerns over the growing number of cases among travellers returning to the United States. The other countries listed are Bolivia, Cambodia, Colombia, Malaysia, the Maldives, New Caledonia, Samoa, Sri Lanka, Tonga and Vietnam. Health experts say dengue fever is transmitted primarily through the bite of infected Aedes aegypti mosquitoes, which thrive in warm, humid environments and commonly breed in stagnant water. More from Kenya 42 minutes ago Xavi Hernández Unveiled as Netherlands Head Coach as Patrick Kluivert Emerges in Coaching Plans 1 hour ago Somalia’s Public Works Minister Meets Investors from Türkiye, Djibouti and Sudan. Symptoms typically appear between three and ten days after infection and may include high fever, severe headaches, muscle and joint pain, skin rashes, nausea and, in some cases, bleeding from the nose or gums. Severe infections can lead to life-threatening complications if not treated promptly. Kenya's current outbreak is believed to have started in late 2025, although the country has experienced several previous outbreaks over the past four decades, particularly in coastal regions. Major outbreaks were reported in the early 1980s, between 2013 and 2014 and again in 2021. Public health officials have long warned that rising temperatures, rapid urbanisation and changing rainfall patterns are creating favourable conditions for the spread of mosquito-borne diseases. Unlike some countries, Kenya has not yet incorporated widespread dengue vaccination into its national immunisation programmes, making prevention and early diagnosis the country's primary line of defence. The latest advisory comes at a time when Kenya is recording increased international arrivals, with tourism authorities continuing efforts to attract more visitors to the country's beaches, wildlife parks and cultural heritage sites. Officials are therefore expected to intensify surveillance and public awareness campaigns in the affected regions. Bangladesh Dengue Outbreak Worsens, Death Toll Rises To 59 As Cases Near 18,000 - Menafn
新闻简报截至 2026 年 8 月 8 日,孟加拉国正经历 重症登革热 暴发,已有 59 人死亡,报告病例 17,880 例。达卡地区受影响最严重,报告了 6,871 例病例,其中仅达卡市就有 4,404 例,死亡人数为 26 人,其中该市有 25 人死亡。暴发 的原因是人口密度高、伊蚊持续繁殖以及 媒介 控制措施不足。专家警告说,暴发 的高峰可能会延续到 9 月份,感染人数仍呈上升趋势。卫生服务总局(DGHS)提供了数据,当地媒体也报道了日益恶化的情况。人们呼吁建立国家蚊虫控制机构来更有效地解决这个问题。反应仍然是被动的,由于该地区的情况,进一步传播的风险很高。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 于 2026 年 8 月恶化,最新报告日期为 2026-08-08。暴发 的峰值预计将持续到 8 月直至 2026 年 9 月。
地点与对象
暴发 主要集中在孟加拉国,特别是达卡省,该省的病例和死亡人数最多。仅达卡市就有4404例感染,受影响人群包括达卡以及巴里沙尔、吉大港、库尔纳等其他地区的居民。
事件
截至 2026 年 8 月 8 日,孟加拉国的登革热 暴发 已导致 59 人死亡,报告病例达 17,880 例。卫生服务总局 (DGHS) 报告了这些数字,当地媒体也报道了日益恶化的情况。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年8月8日,已报告17,880例登革热病例,其中59人死亡。达卡省的病例数(6,871 例)和死亡人数(26 例)最多,仅达卡市就有 4,404 例感染病例。由于人口密度高、蚊子繁殖和控制措施不足,持续传播的风险凸显。
应对、状态与不确定性
专家指出,孟加拉国的蚊虫控制措施主要是反应性的,而不是预防性的。有人呼吁国家蚊虫控制机构协调和监督 媒介 控制措施。目前情况仍在调查中,八月和九月病例和死亡人数可能进一步增加。
