Bangladesh Dengue Outbreak Worsens, Death Toll Rises To 59 As Cases Near 18,000 - Menafn

News Brief
Bangladesh is experiencing a severe dengue outbreak as of August 8, 2026, with 59 deaths and 17,880 reported cases. Dhaka division has been the most affected, recording 6,871 cases, including 4,404 in Dhaka city alone, and 26 fatalities, with 25 in the city. The outbreak is attributed to high population density, persistent Aedes mosquito breeding, and inadequate vector control measures. Experts warn that the peak of the outbreak may extend into September, with continued upward trends in infections. The Directorate General of Health Services (DGHS) has provided the data, and local media have reported on the worsening situation. Calls for a national mosquito control authority have been made to address the issue more effectively. The response remains reactive, and the risk of further spread is high due to the conditions in the region.

Bangladesh Dengue Outbreak Worsens, Death Toll Rises To 59 As Cases Near 18,000 Date 2026-08-08 02:00:31 Share on Facebook Tweet on Twitter (MENAFN- IANS) Dhaka, Aug 8 (IANS) As Bangladesh grapples with a worsening dengue outbreak, at least 59 people have died from the disease, while 17,880 cases have been reported so far this year, local media reported citing data from the country's Directorate General of Health Services (DGHS). According to DGHS data, Dhaka division has emerged as Bangladesh's worst affected region, recording 6,871 cases this year, including 4,404 infections in Dhaka city alone. City & Local Guides With 3,647 cases, Barishal division accounted for the second-highest number of infections, followed by Chattogram with 3,082 and Khulna with 2,272. Additionally, Dhaka division topped the list for dengue fatalities, with 26 deaths, 25 of which were reported in Dhaka city. Khulna recorded the second-highest death toll at 12, followed by Chattogram and Mymensingh with seven and six deaths, respectively, Bangladesh's leading newspaper The Daily Star reported. Dengue, a mosquito-borne viral disease, has emerged as a serious public health concern in Bangladesh. It is primarily transmitted by the Aedes aegypti mosquito. GM Saifur Rahman, Associate Professor in the Department of Zoology at the National University of Bangladesh, said that the latest figure reflects Dhaka's combination of high population density, persistent Aedes mosquito breeding, and inadequate vector control measures. The combination of high population density, warm climate and abundant breeding grounds reportedly provides ideal conditions for both Aedes mosquitoes and the dengue virus to circulate year-round, turning the Dhaka division into a major dengue hotspot. Infectious Diseases “Just a few days ago, while supervising an examination at Central Metropolitan Women's College, I found Aedes mosquito larvae in a small container of water. Such breeding sites are common across the city, indicating that Aedes mosquitoes are reproducing continuously in residential areas,” The Daily Star quoted Saifur as saying. Stressing that the mosquito control measures have been largely reactive rather than preventive, he further said,“The most effective strategy would be to intensify mosquito control during periods when dengue transmission is relatively low. At that time, breeding sites are fewer, and interventions can be more effective. Instead, mosquito control usually intensifies only after the dengue season begins, by which time infections are already increasing rapidly.” According to Saifur, Bangladesh's failure to establish an effective, centralised mosquito control mechanism has worsened the country's dengue situation. He called for the formation of a national mosquito control authority staffed with expert entomologists to coordinate, monitor and oversee vector control measures nationwide. “Until we establish a coordinated national control system, dengue cases are likely to continue rising,” he warned. Infectious Diseases Kabirul Bashar, Professor of the Zoology Department at Jahangirnagar University, warned that dengue infections are expected to surge through August and could continue their upward trajectory into September. “The peak may not occur in August. It's possible that this year's peak will come in September. What we can say is that the upward trend is likely to continue through August and into September,” he said. View five news elements
Time
The outbreak worsened in August 2026, with the latest report dated 2026-08-08. The peak of the outbreak is expected to continue through August and into September 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak is primarily concentrated in Bangladesh, specifically in Dhaka division, which has recorded the highest number of cases and deaths. Dhaka city alone has 4,404 infections, and the affected population includes residents of Dhaka and other regions such as Barishal, Chattogram, and Khulna.
Event
A dengue outbreak in Bangladesh has led to 59 deaths and 17,880 reported cases as of August 8, 2026. The Directorate General of Health Services (DGHS) reported these figures, and local media have covered the worsening situation.
Scale, impact and risk
As of August 8, 2026, 17,880 dengue cases have been reported, with 59 deaths. Dhaka division has the highest number of cases (6,871) and deaths (26), with Dhaka city alone accounting for 4,404 infections. The risk of continued spread is highlighted due to high population density, mosquito breeding, and inadequate control measures.
Response, status and uncertainty
Experts have noted that mosquito control measures in Bangladesh are largely reactive rather than preventive. There is a call for a national mosquito control authority to coordinate and oversee vector control measures. The situation remains under investigation, with the potential for further increases in cases and deaths through August and September.

随着病例上升,美国向肯尼亚发出登革热旅行警报 - Hiiraan Online

US issues dengue travel alert for Kenya as cases rise - Hiiraan Online
新闻简报由于登革热病例上升,美国于2026年8月7日向肯尼亚发布旅行健康警报,敦促旅行者采取预防蚊虫叮咬的预防措施。该警报特别强调肯尼亚沿海地区、瓦吉尔县和加里萨县是值得关注的地区。加里萨卫生当局通过加里萨县转诊医院和国家公共卫生实验室的实验室检测确认了登革热感染,40 个样本中有 28 个检测呈阳性。暴发 已导致患者入院治疗,并需要进行为期两周的熏蒸以消灭伊蚊。美国大使馆建议咨询疾病预防控制中心的旅行健康建议,并使用经美国环保署注册的驱虫剂、穿着防护服并在空调房内睡觉。建议患者监测症状,优先休息,并在出现警告信号时立即寻求医疗救助。登革热是一种由伊蚊传播的病毒感染,伊蚊在死水中繁殖,可引起发烧、疼痛和恶心,并可能导致严重并发症。
查看新闻五要素
时间
随着 登革热 病例增加,美国于 2026 年 8 月 7 日星期五发布了旅行健康警报。加里萨县的熏蒸演习于2026年5月星期四开始,持续了两周。
地点与对象
该警报覆盖肯尼亚沿海地区、瓦吉尔县和加里萨县。受影响的人群包括加里萨县的居民,特别是在加里萨县转诊医院住院的居民。
事件
由于 登革热 病例增加,美国驻内罗毕大使馆发布了针对肯尼亚的旅行健康警报。卫生当局通过加里萨县转诊医院和国家公共卫生实验室的实验室检测确认了登革热感染。
规模、影响与风险
从加里萨的公共和私人卫生机构收集的 40 个样本中,有 28 个样本的 登革热 检测呈阳性。登革热是一种由蚊子传播的病毒,可导致发烧、疼痛、恶心和皮疹,并可能导致严重并发症。该疾病主要通过受感染的伊蚊叮咬传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国大使馆建议旅行者查阅疾病预防控制中心的旅行健康建议,并采取措施避免蚊虫叮咬。加里萨县卫生官员进行了为期两周的熏蒸活动以消灭蚊子。实验室检测证实了暴发,敦促患者监测症状并在必要时寻求医疗帮助。

US issues dengue travel alert for Kenya as cases rise - Hiiraan Online

News Brief
The United States issued a travel health alert for Kenya on August 7, 2026, due to a rise in dengue fever cases, urging travellers to take precautions against mosquito bites. The alert specifically highlights Kenya's coastal region, Wajir, and Garissa counties as areas of concern. Health authorities in Garissa confirmed dengue infections through laboratory tests at the Garissa County Referral Hospital and the National Public Health Laboratory, with 28 out of 40 samples testing positive. The outbreak has led to hospitalisations, prompting a two-week fumigation exercise to eliminate Aedes mosquitoes. The US Embassy recommends consulting the CDC's travel health advisories and using EPA-registered insect repellent, wearing protective clothing, and sleeping in air-conditioned rooms. Patients are advised to monitor symptoms, prioritise rest, and seek immediate medical assistance if warning signs appear. Dengue is a viral infection transmitted by Aedes mosquitoes, which breed in stagnant water, and can cause fever, aches, and nausea, with potential for severe complications.
Original source text (English)
by PERPETUA ETYANG Friday August 7, 2026 Image generated with AI The United States has issued a travel health alert for Kenya following a rise in dengue fever cases, urging travellers to take extra precautions against mosquito bites. In a health alert issued by the US Embassy in Nairobi, the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) listed Kenya among countries reporting a higher number of dengue cases in its updated Travel Health Notice. The advisory identifies Kenya's coastal region, as well as Wajir and Garissa counties, as areas where travellers should exercise caution. "Dengue is a mosquito-borne virus common in tropical and subtropical climates that can lead to fever, aches and pains, nausea, and rashes," the embassy said. The embassy urged travellers to consult the CDC's travel health advisories before travelling to affected areas and to take measures to avoid mosquito bites. Recommended precautions include using an Environmental Protection Agency (EPA)-registered insect repellent, wearing long-sleeved shirts and long trousers when outdoors, and sleeping in air-conditioned rooms or rooms fitted with window screens. The alert comes as health authorities continue to monitor dengue infections in several parts of the country, with the disease primarily transmitted through the bite of infected Aedes mosquitoes. The US Embassy advised travellers seeking more information to consult the CDC's travel health guidance or contact the agency directly before visiting affected areas. Dengue is a viral infection that affects millions of people worldwide and is transmitted through the bite of infected mosquitoes. Once diagnosed, it is important to follow medical advice closely, monitor symptoms, and prioritise rest. Adequate hydration plays a crucial role in preventing complications, while nutritious meals help support the body's recovery process. Patients should remain alert to warning signs that may indicate worsening illness and seek immediate medical assistance when necessary. In some cases, hospitalisation may be recommended to ensure close monitoring and appropriate treatment during the recovery period. In May, Garissa county embarked on a massive fumigation exercise aimed at containing the spread of dengue fever following a recent outbreak that has seen several residents hospitalised at the Garissa County Referral Hospital. The two-week exercise, which officially kicked off on Thursday, will involve public health officials fumigating drainage areas, public institutions, schools, markets and other public spaces across Garissa township in an effort to eliminate mosquitoes responsible for spreading the disease. Health officials said samples taken from patients admitted at the Garissa County Referral Hospital confirmed infections of dengue fever, a viral disease transmitted by the Aedes mosquito, which commonly breeds in stagnant water. Speaking to the press after overseeing the launch of the fumigation exercise, Hassan Abdullahi, who was representing the Medical Officer of Health said laboratory tests conducted at the National Public Health Laboratory confirmed the outbreak after 28 out of 40 samples collected from both public and private health facilities tested positive for dengue fever
View five news elements
Time
The United States issued a travel health alert on Friday August 7, 2026, following a rise in dengue fever cases. The fumigation exercise in Garissa county began on Thursday, May 2026, and lasted for two weeks.
Location and population
The alert covers Kenya's coastal region, Wajir, and Garissa counties. The affected population includes residents of Garissa county, particularly those hospitalised at the Garissa County Referral Hospital.
Event
The US Embassy in Nairobi issued a travel health alert for Kenya due to an increase in dengue fever cases. Health authorities confirmed dengue infections through laboratory tests at the Garissa County Referral Hospital and the National Public Health Laboratory.
Scale, impact and risk
28 out of 40 samples collected from public and private health facilities in Garissa tested positive for dengue fever. Dengue is a mosquito-borne virus that can lead to fever, aches, nausea, and rashes, with potential for severe complications. The disease is primarily transmitted through the bite of infected Aedes mosquitoes.
Response, status and uncertainty
The US Embassy advised travellers to consult the CDC's travel health advisories and take measures to avoid mosquito bites. Health officials in Garissa county conducted a two-week fumigation exercise to eliminate mosquitoes. Laboratory tests confirmed the outbreak, and patients are urged to monitor symptoms and seek medical assistance when necessary.
